FACILITATOR GUIDE: WORKSHOP CREATOR TRAINING

Thursday 8 October 2026, 09:00 to 13:10. By ten past one she will have run a whole workshop on the live system on her own login. She will have created, published and shared a TRAINING workshop, followed a booking, handed a payment to Finance, run check-in, marked attendance, sent a certificate and pulled an export. She will also know the one question that runs the whole day: "Is this mine to do, or do I recognise it and hand it to Finance?"

Her two email addresses

Write these in before the day. Every booking she makes uses one of them, so she receives and sees every email her own bookings send.

- Trainee work email: 
- Trainee personal email: 

Wherever this guide says <Trainee work email> or <Trainee personal email>, it means the address written here.

How to use this guide

- Every block starts with its time and a Goal. Read the goal first. That is what she should walk away knowing, and it is how you judge the block.
- Anything she types, picks or reads on screen sits on its own line with a bold label, like Title: or Short code:. Button and screen names are in double quotes, exactly as they show in the app.
- Read the Cannot be undone line before the block starts. When a block has one, stop before that click and say it out loud with her.

Words you will see

Short code. A code of up to 6 letters or digits that the system builds from the workshop title. It goes into EFT payment references and receipt numbers, so you wait for the word "Available" under it before you save. If you change it later, only new numbers use the new code.

EFT reference. The code the payer must type exactly into their bank's reference box. It is built from the short code, a number the system picks, and up to 5 letters of the surname. For a school or a company it uses letters of its name instead. For example, Jaco van der Merwe on "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after" gets EFT-TRC-(number)-VANDE. It is the same on the success page, in the email and on the staff row. Finance uses it to match the bank statement. Always copy it from 'Payment reference' in "View details".

Workshop creator tick. A tick a super admin puts on a staff account. It lets her create, edit, publish and close workshops, upload and remove workshop files and open check-in early. It is why she can see the "Create Workshop" tab. It also gives her a "Delete workshop" button, but that only works while nobody has paid on the workshop. Once any payment exists, the system refuses and she uses "Close registrations" instead. It does not let her change the reminder rules. In the Western Cape five staff members have this tick.

Finance tick. Finance means anyone with the "Finance" tick, anyone with the auditor role (an auditor always counts as Finance, no tick needed) and a super admin. Only Finance can mark a booking paid, create, edit or switch off coupons, cancel or remove a booking, send a manual payment reminder, correct a payer's email and pull the export with money in it. She does not have this tick, so those buttons are simply not on her screen. That is on purpose, not a fault. In the Western Cape nobody has the "Finance" tick and there is no auditor, so Finance here means the super admins only.

The groups in "All Registrations". This tab sorts a workshop's people into groups:

- "Owes money": the booking exists but is not paid yet. An EFT booking stays here until Finance marks it paid. The row says "Attendance opens once the EFT is marked paid".
- "Check in": paid, and waiting to be marked present on the day.
- "Certificate to send": marked present, certificate not sent yet. Staff can send one at once with "Issue now". If nobody does, it goes out by itself at 08:00 the morning after the workshop's last day.
- "Done": the certificate has been sent, or the person was marked absent. A paid school row also sits here.
- "Cancelled": cancelled bookings. A normal "Cancel booking" deletes the row completely, so you cannot set up a row here on purpose. Finance clears a row from this group with "Remove", either on the row in "Cancelled" or in the Attendee ledger. "Remove" only shows when the booking has no payment history. A row with payment history stays and reads 'Kept for the money record: this booking has a payment history.'

The groups in "Published Links". This tab lists every workshop with its links, QR codes, check-in, files and coupons, sorted into groups:

- "Current & upcoming": published workshops that have not ended. Their booking link works for anyone.
- "Needs closure": the dates have passed but registrations are still open. The count reads "N past dates still open" and "Copy" and "Open" are switched off. The website Events page drops a workshop as soon as its end time passes. The booking link and its QR code still take bookings until someone clicks "Close registrations", or until the nightly run closes the workshop at 00:05. Only a Workshop creator or a super admin can click it.
- "Drafts": created but never published. Nobody outside the office can see or book them. The group reads "Drafts", then how many are awaiting publication.
- "Closed archive": workshops ended with "Close registrations". Every booking, payment, attendance mark, certificate and file stays with them.

Attendee ledger. The full list of bookings inside one workshop. Find it at "Published Links", select the workshop, open 'Registrations, coupons and documents', then 'Attendee ledger'. Staff use it to fix a wrong attendance mark after the "Undo" button has gone. Finance can "Remove" a cancelled row here too.

School code. When a school pays once for several places, Finance marks that payment paid. Only then does the school's receipt email carry a school code and a "Register a teacher" button. Each teacher uses it to register on a place at R 0,00. A member may take a non-member place. A non-member can never take a member place. In the school booking's details, each teacher who used the code is listed under "Used the school's code:".

CPTD points. Continuing Professional Teacher Development points, the points a teacher earns for attending. They are printed on the certificate and frozen there. In the "CPTD points" tab she searches a surname and clicks the row to read the date, points, SACE number and certificate number. The tab groups people by the member number they booked with.

TRAINING workshop. A practice workshop with a title like "TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after". 'Delete after' means we clean it up when the day is over. A Workshop creator or a super admin can press "Delete workshop", but only while nobody has paid on it. It asks for a reason and cannot be undone. Once any payment exists, delete is refused, so we press "Close registrations" and the workshop moves to "Closed archive" with everything kept.

Automatic certificates. A certificate goes out by itself at 08:00 the morning after a workshop's last day. It goes to everyone who paid and was marked present, once only. Nobody who is unpaid, absent or not marked gets one. It covers workshops that end after 03:24 on 08 Oct 2026, when a super admin switched it on, so today's training workshops are covered. Staff can still send one person's certificate straight away with "Issue now".

Automatic registration close. Live since 04:35 on 08 Oct 2026. Every night at 00:05 SAST the system closes every published workshop whose end time was before midnight. The first run is the night of 8 to 9 October. A closed workshop refuses new bookings. An unpaid booking on it can no longer get EFT details or pay by card, and its payment reminders stop. Until that night's run, an ended workshop still sits in "Needs closure" and its link still takes bookings, so today's "Needs closure" example still works and she can close a workshop herself as soon as it is over. Pressing "Reopen registrations" on a workshop whose dates have passed is undone at the next 00:05 run. If the event moved, edit the dates instead.

Training email addresses. Every booking she makes uses her own address, <Trainee work email> or <Trainee personal email>, so she receives and sees every email it sends. Every booking you make uses loxly@lbtec.co.za. The one deliberately wrong address is wrong.address@example.com. The system emails only the address on the booking, so a stranger's address would send real receipts and reminders to a real person. When an address is used again on the same workshop, the system shows "Possible existing registration" with the reason "same email, same telephone". Click "Save as additional entry".

The day at a glance

1. 09:00 · Opening briefing: the one-page brief, the anchor question, her role and the four safety rules (4 min)
2. 09:04 · Before she looks: she writes how a workshop goes from idea to certificate and circles the money steps (4 min)
3. 09:08 · She finds her way around the six tabs, then the "Who is Finance?" role-play with Mariam (7 min)
4. 09:15 · Short brief: create, publish, share (3 min)
5. 09:18 · She plans it herself. From her workshop planning sheet she creates "TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after" (15 min)
6. 09:33 · She finishes the details, publishes and shares with Mr Deon Pretorius, then uploads the poster and agenda (9 min)
7. 09:42 · Quick quiz on setting up a workshop (3 min)
8. 09:45 · She opens the workshop's booking link in a private window (2 min)
9. 09:47 · She books Sipho Ndlovu as a non-member, then takes his worried phone call (12 min)
10. 09:59 · She books Refilwe Moloi at the member rate (6 min)
11. 10:05 · Coupons: you create two as Finance, she uses them (5 min)
12. 10:10 · Role-play: Mrs Elmarie Pienaar books three places for Bergrivier Primary School (13 min)
13. 10:23 · Quick quiz on bookings (2 min)
14. 10:25 · Break (15 min)
15. 10:40 · She finds Sipho's unpaid booking (2 min)
16. 10:42 · She reads who owes money, the reminder schedule and the bank details, look only (6 min)
17. 10:48 · Role-play: Jaco van der Merwe says "I paid on Monday" (12 min)
18. 11:00 · Role-play: Nomvula Dlamini says "Stop reminding me" (6 min)
19. 11:06 · Go or stop check: do it, hand it to Finance, or stop and escalate (4 min)
20. 11:10 · She raises a price, checks Sipho's amount did not change, then closes and reopens (2 min)
21. 11:12 · Role-play: Mrs Fatima Salie and the workshop that moved (13 min)
22. 11:25 · She books as Busisiwe Nkosi with a wrong email, and you fix, remind and cancel it as Finance (10 min)
23. 11:35 · Go or stop check: problems that belong to Finance (5 min)
24. 11:40 · Break (10 min)
25. 11:50 · She opens today's workshop in "All Registrations" and reads the summary line (2 min)
26. 11:52 · The door QR and check-in, then the morning email to attendees (7 min)
27. 11:59 · Role-play: Mrs Charmaine Adonis at the door, keeping the register (12 min)
28. 12:11 · Role-play: Thandi Mokoena wants her certificate (10 min)
29. 12:21 · She exports the register and reads one person's CPTD points (2 min)
30. 12:23 · Quick quiz on the workshop day (2 min)
31. 12:25 · Capstone brief and checklist (2 min)
32. 12:27 · Capstone: she runs the CEO's workshop from first click to closed record (25 min)
33. 12:52 · Go or stop check: three last questions (3 min)
34. 12:55 · Debrief and learning record (15 min)

Before she arrives

Wednesday 7 October, evening (about 2 hours 30 minutes)

Do every step as super admin at https://naptosawc.co.za/staff unless the step says private window. Make every booking from the workshop's public booking link in a private browser window, and always pick EFT. Never type a card number. Every booking you make uses loxly@lbtec.co.za. From the second booking with that address on the same workshop, the system shows "Possible existing registration" with the reason "same email, same telephone". Click "Save as additional entry".

Read the plan (5 min)

1. Read "The day at a glance" once, from 09:00 to 13:10.
2. On your copy, mark what you drop first if you run late, in this order:
   - 11:25, the wrong-email booking as Busisiwe Nkosi
   - 10:05, the coupons
   - 11:00, the "Stop reminding me" call from Nomvula Dlamini
   - 09:59, the member booking for Refilwe Moloi
   - 12:21, the export
   - 12:52, the last go or stop check
3. Mark what you never drop: the 09:18 workshop she creates from her planning sheet, the 09:47 booking with Sipho's call, the 10:10 school booking, the 10:48 "I paid on Monday" call, the 11:59 door scene, the 12:11 certificate call, the capstone and the learning record.

Check her account (5 min)

4. Go to Settings > "Roles & Permissions" > "Staff Access Management" > "Provincial Roles".
5. Find her row. Check that "Workshop creator" is ticked, "Finance" is not ticked and the province is Western Cape. Change nothing. Five Western Cape rows have "Workshop creator" ticked. Note on your card whose rows they are, so you recognise them tomorrow.
6. Look down the "Finance" column. When this guide was checked, nobody in the Western Cape had the "Finance" tick and there was no auditor row. If that is still true, write on your card:
   - Finance in the Western Cape: the super admins only
   She copies this onto her cheat sheet at 09:08.

Test values to prepare

Write all of these on YOUR copies only. Her handouts carry no ID, PERSAL, NAP or cell number.

Email addresses. Three kinds, no others:
- Bookings she makes: <Trainee work email> or <Trainee personal email>, as listed per booking above. She receives and sees every email they send.
- Bookings you make: loxly@lbtec.co.za
- The deliberately wrong address: wrong.address@example.com, for Busisiwe Nkosi at 11:25 only

One exception on school places: a teacher registering on a school's paid place cannot use an email that already has a paid booking on that workshop. The page refuses it with "This email already has a paid booking for this workshop." So Anita Fourie uses <Trainee personal email>, Xolani Ngcobo uses <Trainee work email> and Lerato Jantjies uses <Trainee personal email>. Bongani Mabaso's free booking uses <Trainee work email>, so Anita's address carries no paid booking on that workshop.

Reusing an address on the same workshop shows "Possible existing registration" with the reason "same email, same telephone". Click "Save as additional entry".

Finance names. From step 6. Nobody in the Western Cape has the "Finance" tick and there is no auditor, so:
- Finance in the Western Cape: the super admins only

She writes this on her cheat sheet at 09:08.

Test member numbers. One per member booking, numbered 01 to 09:
- 01: Refilwe Moloi
- 02: Bongani Mabaso
- 03: Nomvula Dlamini
- 04: Lungile Mkhize
- 05: Busisiwe Nkosi
- 06: Thandi Mokoena
- 07: Palesa Khoza
- 08: Naledi Shabalala
- 09: Sizwe Dube

Start from the NAP number on a test member record that you control, never a real member's. Add -T01 to -T09 on the end. If you have no such record, use TRN-NAP-01 to TRN-NAP-09. The letters mean it can never be a real NAP number. Check that no value has exactly 13 digits once the letters are taken out, because 13 digits trigger the SA ID check. Keep them all different, because the "CPTD points" tab groups people by the number they booked with.

Made-up SA ID numbers. Three valid ones and one invalid one:
- Valid ID 1: Anita Fourie, 10:10
- Valid ID 2: Lerato Jantjies, 11:59
- Valid ID 3: Xolani Ngcobo, tonight only
- Invalid ID: typed into Refilwe Moloi's booking at 09:59 to show the error, never saved

Build each valid number from 13 digits: 000101 (a birth date of 1 January 2000), then 4 sequence digits (different for each of the three), then 0, then 8, then one check digit. Pick the check digit with the Luhn rule, the same rule the app uses: try each digit from 0 to 9 until the whole 13-digit number passes the Luhn check. A short Python loop does this. For the invalid one, take valid ID 1 and change only the last digit (add 1, and 9 becomes 0). The app must answer 'That looks like an SA ID number but the check digit does not match...'. Search CRM contacts for each number first and expect zero hits.

Test cell numbers. A South African mobile number in the right format (10 digits starting with 06, 07 or 08) for each individual booking. Use numbers you control where you can.
- 1: Sipho
- 2: Refilwe
- 3: Carmen
- 4: Bongani
- 5: Jaco
- 6: Nomvula
- 7: not used
- 8: Lungile
- 9: Busisiwe
- 10: Thandi
- 11: Hennie
- 12: Palesa
- 13: Naledi
- 14: Aisha
- 15: Sizwe

All 14 must be different, because the duplicate check also matches on cell number. Search CRM contacts for each one and expect zero hits, because the booking sync matches contacts by cell number first. School bookings and teachers on school places need none.

PERSAL. Leave the optional PERSAL box empty. It is never needed today. Never type a real one.

The two coupons. A coupon is a discount code that only Finance can create, edit or switch off. You create both live at 10:05 on "TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after", so neither exists at 09:00. After the day, switch both off as Finance with "Switch off". Only the number of people and the note can be edited. The code, price and "Applies to" cannot. Switch off is refused while an unpaid booking still holds the code, for example Carmen's until it is paid or cancelled. Add both to the clean-up list.

Every hit sits on a TRAINING workshop, and no row on any other workshop carries loxly@lbtec.co.za. No real workshop was opened in "Edit details", published, closed or given a coupon or a file.
- Published Links at 08:45: "Drafts" holds "TRAINING - Short Code Clash - delete after" (plus any real drafts, untouched). "Needs closure" holds Term 3 Curriculum Planning. Classroom Discipline and Capstone Run do not exist yet.

"Done" holds the Silvertree row with 1 of 2 places used. The check-in email has not been sent, nobody is marked, no certificate has been issued, and "CPTD points" shows nobody for this workshop.

- Payment settings, look only: 'What members see now' shows a confirmed bank account. It is the training placeholder (ABSA, account 12345678910, branch 123456) that nobody pays. 'Final EFT reminder, 2 days before' is On. You know whether the starting-soon and attendance emails are On or Off. Nothing was saved on either page.
- Card safety: on every TRAINING booking page the card option reads 'Card (demonstration)', and nobody uses it. No real card number is written or typed anywhere.
- Test values: every value is filled in on your copies only. Each made-up valid ID passes the Luhn check and the invalid one fails it. CRM contact searches for every cell number and made-up ID returned zero hits. Her handouts carry no ID, PERSAL, NAP or cell number.
- Files: the four pictures and the agenda PDF are on her desktop, each marked 'TRAINING - delete after' and under 25 MB.
- Receipts list: 5 rows from Classroom Management Essentials (Thandi, Hennie, Palesa, Naledi, Silvertree) with their invoice and receipt numbers. Finance has been told in writing that more will follow.
- "Remove" is taught in words only: at the 11:35 go or stop check. A normal "Cancel booking" deletes the row, so you cannot set up a row in "Cancelled" on purpose. Finance's "Remove" sits on rows in the "Cancelled" group and in the Attendee ledger, and only for bookings with no payment history.
- Not seeded on purpose: no bookings for Petersen, Sithebe, Mitchells Plain Primary School or Fortuin, and no "Booking Practice" or "Full House Test" workshop.
- The room: your super admin screen is out of her reach, the mail client shows loxly@lbtec.co.za, her laptop shows her own two inboxes, the phone is charged and on Wi-Fi, notifications are muted and the anchor question is taped up.
- The Events page: every published Western Cape workshop shows on the public Events page with a Register button, TRAINING ones included. Publish TRAINING workshops as late as you can and close them straight after the session. A workshop drops off the Events page by itself once its end time passes, and its registration closes by itself at 00:05 that night. Until then the booking link and its QR code keep taking bookings, unless someone clicks "Close registrations".
- The Silvertree link: open the Silvertree receipt email (loxly@lbtec.co.za) on your phone and keep its "Register a teacher" link ready. Do not submit it.

Your first five minutes

Say these in your own words. The times are rough.

1. Welcome (30 seconds). "Good morning, and thank you for taking this on. By ten past one you will have run a whole workshop on the real system, from the first click to the closed record. Today is about doing, not listening, so your hands will be on the keyboard most of the morning."
2. Why it matters (30 seconds). "Members book, pay and get CPTD certificates through this module. When you run it well, a teacher books in two minutes and gets her certificate the same day. When a step is missed, someone gets chased for money they already paid."
3. Where she starts (30 seconds). "Before I show you anything: have you used the Workshops page before, or booked a workshop on our website yourself?" Listen. Use her words for things from now on.
4. The anchor question (30 seconds). Point to the board. "This is the question for the whole day: is this mine to do, or do I recognise it and hand it to Finance? Your account has the Workshop creator tick and not the Finance tick, so some buttons will simply not be on your screen. That is by design, not a fault."
5. Safety (1 minute). "We are on the live system. Four rules. We only use workshops called TRAINING, dash, topic, dash, delete after. Every booking you make uses your own work or personal email, so you see every email it sends. Every booking I make uses my address. We pay by EFT only, never with a real card. And nothing that is marked paid, emailed or certified can be taken back, so before those clicks we stop and say it out loud."
6. What she will do (30 seconds). "We follow one workshop's life in order: create, publish, share, follow bookings, chase payment, handle changes, run the day, attendance, certificates, exports. At the end you run one on your own while I play the CEO."
7. How the role-plays work (30 seconds). "Sometimes I will play a caller. When I play a character, I stay in character. When I say Narrator, I am telling you what happens on screen. When I say Coach, I am me again. The hint slips stay face down. Turn one over whenever you want."
8. Predict before she sees (hand over at 09:04). "Before we open anything, write on this sheet how you think a workshop goes from idea to certificate, in your own words. Then circle the steps you think are money steps. There is no wrong answer. We will compare it at the end." Then stop talking and let her write for 4 minutes.

How to run a role-play

Prep (1 to 2 minutes, as Coach)
- Read her learner card out loud: the situation, who the caller is, her role ("you are the Workshop creator, you do not have the Finance tick") and which screen she starts on.
- Say any "this cannot be undone" warning here, never inside the scene.
- Ask: "Ready?"
- Prep has no task and no tactics. Tactics live on the hint slips.

Play (2 to 4 parts)
- Open each part with its narrator line if it has one ("Narrator: the phone rings."), then say the character's opening line.
- She answers in her own words or does it in the app.
- Watch for the "done well when" sign on your card. It is something concrete she says or does, like "she says she cannot mark it paid herself and names who can".
- If the slot is under 12 minutes, run 2 parts, not 4. Pick the part with the hand-off decision and the part with the screen action.

Staying in character
- Speak only as the person, in the first person, with short replies a real person would give.
- Chase the character's own goal. A worried member wants her seat. A busy CEO wants a number.
- Never praise, grade, explain or hint while in character.
- If she gets something wrong, live the result: "So my booking is just gone?"
- Give away a hidden fact only when she asks the right question, for example the EFT reference the member used. Never put it in your opening line.
- Never invent a button, email or automatic step. If she asks the character something you are not sure the app does, the character says "I don't know, you tell me". Check the cheat sheet after the scene.
- If she asks "is this the real system?", answer as the character would: "It's what I see on my side, yes."

The Narrator hat
- Say "Narrator:" for anything that happens off-stage, like "Narrator: the member has just booked" or "Narrator: Finance has marked it paid."
- Then do the Finance click on YOUR screen and let her see the result on hers after she presses "Refresh".
- Keep narration to 1 or 2 neutral sentences. Never sum up what the character said.

The Coach hat
- Say "Coach:" before you step out of the scene.
- Use it only in prep, in the wrap-up, or to stop for safety, for example when she is about to type a member's real email address.

When to give a hint
- Wait for a real stall, not a pause. She says she is stuck, asks for help, or sits for about a minute without moving.
- Then go one step at a time:
  1. Point her to the face-down hint slip.
  2. Give a partial explanation as Coach.
  3. Work through the first half with her, then say "now you try the next bit".
- Hints say how to think ("decide whose job this is before you answer"), never what to say.
- If she says "can you just do it?", the answer is no. Offer a smaller first step or read the screen with her. Her hands stay on her keyboard.

Catch me out, once a morning
- Before a Finance-only moment, act out the wrong move once so she can stop you: "So I just press 'Cancel booking' myself, right?"

Wrap-up (1 to 2 minutes, as Coach)
- Name one good moment tied to something she did, and ONE thing to improve.
- The full review per goal waits for the 12:55 debrief.
- Write achieved, partly or missed for each part on your scoring sheet straight away.

Between scenes and tight slots
- Reset the screens before you start the next scene.
- Three slots are tight: 09:08, 09:33 and 11:52. Expect to use the "If you are running late" line in those blocks.

PART 1: ORIENTATION, THE MAP AND THE RULES (09:00 TO 09:15)

In these 15 minutes she learns where everything lives and which jobs are hers. Nothing in this part can break anything. She looks, writes and talks. She never presses a money button and never touches your screen.

09:00 · Welcome, the one-page brief and the four practice rules (4 min)

Goal: She learns the one question she will ask all day: "Is this mine to do, or do I recognise it and hand it to Finance?" She now knows the four practice rules and why they matter: on the live system a payment mark, an email or a certificate cannot be taken back.

Who drives: Nobody touches a screen. You talk from this page. She listens, reads along and writes in her workbook.

Before you start:
- The one-page brief is on her desk, face up.
  - Brief title: Run a workshop from first draft to certificate today
- The printed cheat sheet is beside her keyboard, open at Stage 1.
- Her workbook and a pen are ready.
- Her printed "Workshop planning sheet" is in her pile, face down. She uses it later, when she creates her own workshop.
- Her two email addresses are written on the first page of this guide:
  - Her work email: <Trainee work email>
  - Her personal email: <Trainee personal email>
- The anchor question is written on the board: "Is this mine to do, or do I recognise it and hand it to Finance?"

What happens:
1. You say: "Good morning, and thank you for taking this on. By ten past one you will have run a whole workshop on the real system, from the first click to the closed record. Today is about doing, not listening, so your hands will be on the keyboard most of the morning."
2. You say: "Members book, pay and get CPTD certificates through this module. When you run it well, a teacher books in two minutes and has her certificate in her inbox the next morning. When a step is missed, someone gets chased for money they already paid."
   - CPTD points are the professional development points teachers earn by attending workshops like these. The certificate is their proof of attendance.
3. You ask: "Before I show you anything: have you used the Workshops page before, or booked a workshop on our website yourself?"
4. You listen. From now on, use her words for things.
5. You hand her the brief and point at the cheat sheet.
6. You point to the board and read the anchor question aloud: "Is this mine to do, or do I recognise it and hand it to Finance?"
7. She writes the anchor question at the top of her workbook.
8. You read her role line: "Your account has the 'Workshop creator' tick and does NOT have the 'Finance' tick. So some buttons will simply not be on your screen. That is by design, not a fault."
   - A tick is a permission a super admin switches on for a staff account. You (Loxly) are the super admin today.
   - "Workshop creator" tick: she can create, edit, publish and close workshops, upload and remove files and open check-in early. It is why she sees the "Create Workshop" tab. She can also delete a workshop for good with "Delete workshop", but only while nobody has paid. It does not let her edit the reminder rules.
   - "Finance" tick: the money jobs. Mark a booking paid, create, edit or switch off coupons, cancel or remove a booking, send a payment reminder by hand, correct a payer's email and pull the export that has money in it.
   - In the Western Cape nobody has the "Finance" tick today and there is no auditor. So Finance here means the super admins.
9. You read the end task: "By 13:10 you will have created, published and shared a TRAINING workshop, followed a booking, handed a payment to Finance, run check-in, marked attendance, sent a certificate and pulled an export."
10. You say the order of the day: "We follow one workshop's life in order. You create it yourself from your own planning sheet, then publish, share, follow bookings, chase payment, handle changes, run the day, attendance, certificates, exports. At the end you run one on your own while I play the CEO."
11. You read the four practice rules:
    - Rule 1, which workshop: only touch workshops titled "TRAINING - <topic> - delete after". Nothing else.
    - Rule 2, which email: every booking she makes uses her own email. Every booking you make uses yours. Never a real member's email.
      - Her bookings: <Trainee work email> or <Trainee personal email>
      - Your bookings: loxly@lbtec.co.za
      - When a block asks for a wrong address on purpose: wrong.address@example.com
      - She receives and reads every email her own bookings send, so she sees exactly what a member sees. No real member gets anything.
      - If she books the same workshop twice with the same email, the system shows "Possible existing registration" with the reason "same email, same telephone". She clicks "Save as additional entry".
    - Rule 3, how they pay: EFT only. EFT is a plain bank transfer into NAPTOSA's account.
      - The bank details on the EFT instructions are for training only. Never pay them.
    - Rule 4, cards: never type a real card number anywhere, not even on the "Card (demonstration)" option.
12. You read why the rules exist. These are the exact warnings she will meet later:
    - On the "Mark EFT paid" box: "A receipt is emailed immediately and this cannot be undone."
    - On certificates: "This cannot be undone."
    - Every email the system sends is a real email.
13. You say: "So before any of those clicks, we stop and say it out loud."
14. You say: "One thing that catches people. There is a 'Delete workshop' button, but it only works while nobody has paid. Once a booking on your TRAINING workshop is marked paid today, it can't be deleted. 'Delete after' in the title means we press 'Close registrations' when the day ends."
    - "Close registrations" stops the booking link working, takes the workshop off the website Events page and moves it to the "Closed archive" list.
    - Registration now closes by itself, but not straight away. Once the workshop's end time passes, the Events page drops it on its own. The booking link, and its QR code, keeps taking bookings until 00:05 that night, when the nightly run closes every workshop that ended before midnight. Until then the workshop waits in the "Needs closure" group, and she can close it sooner with "Close registrations".
15. You explain the role-plays: "Sometimes I will play a caller, like a member or a school asking about a booking or a payment, or a colleague. When I play a character, I stay in character. When I say Narrator, I am telling you what happens on screen. When I say Coach, I am me again. The hint slips stay face down. Turn one over whenever you want."
16. She says the four practice rules back to you in her own words, without looking.

She should see:
- The anchor question written at the top of her workbook.
- The cheat sheet beside her keyboard, open at Stage 1.
- She can say all four practice rules without looking.

If it goes wrong:
- She wants to use a real member's email to "make it realistic": no. The system emails the booking's own address only. A real address means a real person gets receipts and reminders. Her own bookings use her own email, so she sees every email herself:
  - Her bookings: <Trainee work email> or <Trainee personal email>
  - Your bookings: loxly@lbtec.co.za
- She asks how to delete the practice workshop afterwards: once anyone has paid, she cannot. "Delete workshop" only works while nobody has paid. After a payment the box says: "A payment has been recorded for this workshop, so it cannot be deleted. Payment records are always kept. Cancel the workshop instead. To stop new bookings now, use Close registrations." Teach "Close registrations". The other status buttons are "Publish workshop", "Reopen registrations" and "Reopen cancelled workshop".
- She asks whether the workshop closes by itself the day after: yes, since today. At 00:05 every night the system closes every published workshop whose end time was before midnight, and the first run is tonight, the night of 8 to 9 October. A closed workshop refuses new bookings, an unpaid booking on it can no longer get EFT details or pay by card, and its payment reminders stop. Until that run the booking link still takes bookings, so she can close it herself as soon as it is over. If she presses "Reopen registrations" on a workshop whose dates have passed, the next 00:05 run closes it again, so if the event moved she edits the dates instead.
- She asks what happens if she presses a money button by accident: she cannot, because those buttons are not on her screen. The risk sits on your super admin screen. That is why she never drives your screen.
- She asks if a paid mark, a sent email or a certificate can be undone: no. None of that is built, and receipts cannot be deleted either. The only protection is prevention: the TRAINING workshop, her own email or yours on every booking, EFT only, never a real card. If something real goes wrong, she stops and tells you.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block.

If you are running late: Read only the anchor question and the four practice rules. The "why" is in the brief she keeps.

09:04 · Try it before you look: her first version on paper (4 min)

Goal: She learns that she already knows more than she thinks, by putting her own first version of a workshop's life on paper before you show her anything. At the end of the day she compares it with what she can actually do and sees how far she came.

Who drives: She does, on paper. No screens. You stay quiet.

Before you start:
- A blank page in her workbook.
- A pen.

What happens:
1. You say: "Before we open anything: on this sheet, write how you think a workshop goes from idea to certificate, in your own words. Then circle the steps you think are money steps. There is no wrong answer. We will compare it at the end."
2. You stop talking.
3. She writes the steps in her own words, in the order she thinks they happen. Give her about 3 to 4 minutes and say nothing.
4. She circles every step she thinks involves money.
5. She writes one question she hopes today answers, at the bottom of the page.
6. She folds the page and puts it in the back of the workbook.
   - Label on the page: My first version
7. You do not correct it. You put it away until the end-of-day debrief.

She should see:
- A list of steps in her own handwriting.
- Some steps circled as money steps.
- One open question at the bottom.
- The page folded away, uncorrected.

If she gets stuck:
- She freezes on a blank page: ask only "What is the very first thing that happens when the office decides to run a workshop?" Then step back.

If it goes wrong:
- She asks "Is this right?": say "We will check it together at 13:00. Your version is the point."
- She writes a very short list: that is fine. A short first version makes the comparison at the end clearer.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block.

If you are running late: Cut the writing to 3 minutes. Never skip it. The debrief at 12:55 compares against this page.

09:08 · Find the screen, then "Who is Finance?" (7 min)

This block has three parts, back to back:
1. She clicks through the six tabs on her own screen and notices what is missing (about 3 minutes).
2. You show her who counts as Finance on your screen (the first minute of the role-play).
3. The role-play: a helpful colleague offers to sort everything out (about 4 minutes in total, including part 2).

Goal: She learns what each of the six tabs is for, and she realises the money buttons are missing from her screen on purpose: a missing button is not a fault, it tells her who to call. She now knows exactly who counts as Finance (the "Finance" tick, the auditor role or a super admin, nobody else, however senior) and she knows the names of the people she hands money jobs to.

Who drives:
- Part 1: her screen. She clicks.
- Parts 2 and 3: your screen on the second monitor. Only you click. She reads and talks. She never touches your screen.

Before you start:
- She is signed in on her own staff login.
- This practice workshop is already created and published. You did this the night before:
  - Workshop: "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after"
  - Her own TRAINING workshop does not exist yet. She creates it in the next part of the day, from her own printed "Workshop planning sheet".
- The 12 action cards are on the table.
- Your super admin screen is on the second monitor. Have this page ready to open:
  - Path: Settings > "Roles & Permissions" > "Staff Access Management" > "Provincial Roles"
- Your facilitator card with the hidden facts for the role-play (below).
- The names of the super admins, written down ready. Fill these in before the day. In the Western Cape nobody has the "Finance" tick and there is no auditor, so Finance here is the super admins only.
- The Western Cape staff with the "Workshop creator" tick, so you recognise the rows:
  - Workshop creators: the five Western Cape rows you noted on your card the night before
- The hint slips for the role-play, face down beside her.

You play: Mariam, a case admin in the Western Cape office. She has been at NAPTOSA several years longer than the trainee. Warm, quick and a bit proud of her seniority. She wants to save everyone time. She speaks in short, friendly, slightly hurried sentences. She genuinely wants to help and assumes a senior role can do anything. She arrives with a mug of tea and a list of offers from herself and two colleagues, Riaan and Thabo, and she wants a quick yes so she can tell them.
- A case admin is a staff role. It is not a Finance role.

Open with: "Morning! Good news, Kagiso's EFT is on the statement. Riaan says he'll mark him paid himself, he's allowed to, isn't he?"

Keep these to yourself until she asks:
- Riaan's role is auditor, and nobody has ticked Finance on his row. Say it only if she asks what Riaan's role is or how he is set up.
- Mariam herself is a case admin with no Finance tick. Say it only if she asks whether Mariam has the Finance tick. Then Mariam says: "No, but I'm a case admin."
- Mariam wants to change the first EFT reminder to 10 days, just because of this one booking. Say it only if she asks what exactly Mariam wants to change, or why.
- Thabo's account is read-only. That means he can look at everything but change nothing. Say it only if she asks what Thabo's role or access is.

What happens:

Part 1. The six tabs, on her screen

1. She opens the staff login page and signs in with her own staff login.
   - Web address: https://naptosawc.co.za/staff
2. She opens the Workshops page.
   - Web address: https://naptosawc.co.za/staff/operations/workshops
3. She clicks "Create Workshop".
4. She says what it is for: building a new workshop.
5. You tell her: "You only see this tab because of your Workshop creator tick. Don't type in it yet."
6. You warn her: "One thing that catches people. If you press Enter in a one-line box on this form, it creates or saves the workshop. So never use Enter to move between boxes."
7. She clicks "Published Links".
8. She says what it is for: every workshop, with its links, QR codes, check-in, files and coupons. The workshops sit in four groups:
   - "Current & upcoming": workshops still to happen.
   - "Closed archive": workshops where registrations were closed.
   - "Drafts": workshops never published.
   - "Needs closure": workshops whose date has passed but which are still open. The nightly run closes them at 00:05, or someone closes them sooner with "Close registrations".
9. She selects the practice workshop.
   - Workshop: "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after"
10. She opens "Registrations, coupons and documents" on that workshop.
11. She looks at "Coupons" and reads the line on screen aloud.
    - On screen: "Only a super admin, an auditor or a Finance user can create coupons."
    - A coupon is a discount code a person types when they book. Making one is a Finance job.
12. She clicks "All Registrations".
13. She says what it is for: every booking, grouped by what needs doing.
14. She looks at any booking row and notices what is not there:
    - Missing on her screen: "Mark EFT paid", "Remind" and "Cancel booking".
    - "Mark EFT paid" is what Finance presses once a member's bank transfer shows on the statement. It emails a receipt straight away.
15. She clicks "Analytics".
16. She says what it is for: reporting she can read but not change.
    - Examples on screen: "Payment completion", "Revenue collected" and "Attendance".
17. She clicks "Reminders".
18. She says what it is for: the schedule of automatic reminder emails.
    - She lands on "Reminder settings". The button for sending a payment reminder by hand ("Send an EFT payment reminder now") is Finance-only, so it is not on her screen.
    - She looks only. She presses no "Save" here.
19. She clicks "CPTD points".
20. She says what it is for: the points each attendee earned, with their SACE number and certificate number, for the Western Cape. SACE is the South African Council for Educators, the body every teacher is registered with.
21. She clicks "Refresh" at the end of the tab bar. On a smaller screen it shows only as a circular arrows icon.
    - It reloads the workshop list only. Nothing else.
22. She looks at your super admin screen on the second monitor.
    - Your workshop cards carry a province label and show every province.
    - Hers show the Western Cape only, with no province label.
    - Everyone except a super admin sees one province only. Anything she creates is always a Western Cape workshop.
23. (Optional. Skip it if time is short. She then does it later from her workbook.) She sorts the 12 action cards into two piles, "Mine" and "Finance".
    - Mine (5 cards):
      - "Publish workshop": puts the workshop live so people can book.
      - "Close registrations": stops bookings and moves the workshop to "Closed archive". The nightly run also does it at 00:05 the night after the workshop ends, but she can do it sooner.
      - "Present": marks a person as having attended.
      - "Send certificate": emails the person their CPTD certificate. Certificates also go out by themselves at 08:00 the morning after the workshop, to everyone who paid and was marked present.
      - "Export Excel" (no money): downloads a spreadsheet of the bookings without amounts.
    - Finance (7 cards):
      - "Mark EFT paid": confirms a bank transfer arrived and emails a receipt.
      - "Create coupon": makes a discount code. Editing or switching off a coupon is Finance too.
      - "Cancel booking": cancels someone's booking.
      - "Remove": takes away a booking that is already cancelled, only when it has no payment history.
      - "Remind": sends one person a payment reminder by hand.
      - "Correct payer email": fixes the email address on a booking.
      - "Export Excel" (full, with money): the same spreadsheet, with amounts.
    - The two "Export Excel" cards are the same button on screen. On her account the file has no money in it, and its file name says no-financial-data. Finance gets the full file with amounts.

Part 2. Who is Finance, on your screen (the first minute of the role-play)

24. You say the rule once: "Finance is the Finance tick, OR the auditor role, OR a super admin. Nothing else."
25. You open this page on your own screen. She does not open it herself.
    - Path: Settings > "Roles & Permissions" > "Staff Access Management" > "Provincial Roles"
26. She reads the section text on your screen aloud:
    - On screen: "Add workshop rights on top of a person's role. Workshop creator can create, edit and publish workshops. Finance can mark bookings paid, send payment reminders and manage coupons. Only super admins and Finance can mark paid. Marking attendance needs neither tick: every staff member of the province can do it. Each person works in their own province and sees a change after reloading the page."
27. You point at the "Finance" column and say: "Nobody in the Western Cape has this ticked."
28. You say the auditor line in words, because there is no auditor row to show: "If someone here had the auditor role, their row would carry the line 'The Auditor role is always Finance.' We have no auditor in the Western Cape, so there is nobody like that to show you."
29. You point out that super admins are not in the list at all, because they always have both ticks.
30. She finds her own row.
    - Her row: "Workshop creator" ticked. "Finance" not ticked.
    - The other Western Cape rows with "Workshop creator" ticked belong to her fellow Western Cape workshop creators.
31. She writes on her cheat sheet who does Finance in the Western Cape: the super admins only (you, Loxly, and the other names you give her). These are the people she hands money jobs to.
    - Nobody ticks or unticks anything. Not now, not in the role-play.

Part 3. The role-play: a helpful colleague offers to sort everything out

32. You say "Coach:" and read her brief: "You are the Workshop creator for the Western Cape. You have the Workshop creator tick and not the Finance tick. A friendly colleague from the office comes to help with a teacher's payment and some arrangements for the day. Decide, for each offer, whose job it really is."
33. You set the scene: "It's the Western Cape office, mid-morning. A teacher's EFT has shown on the bank statement for a booking on the TRAINING workshop. Mariam stops at your desk with a mug of tea and a list of offers."
34. She puts her own screen on "All Registrations".
35. You (as Mariam) say the opening line: "Morning! Good news, Kagiso's EFT is on the statement. Riaan says he'll mark him paid himself, he's allowed to, isn't he?"
36. She asks what Riaan's role is.
37. You (as Mariam) answer: "He's an auditor." If she asks, add that nobody ticked Finance on his row.
38. She answers Mariam.
    - She does well when: she says Riaan can mark it paid from his own login, because the auditor role is always Finance. No tick needed.
    - While she thinks, Mariam stays cheerful and waits.
    - If she says "No, only I can" or "Only people with the Finance tick", Mariam looks puzzled: "Oh? He thought he could." Move on and pick it up in the debrief. Do not confirm or correct her while you are Mariam.
39. You say: "Narrator: On the second screen, Loxly points at the Finance column. Nobody in the Western Cape has it ticked and there is no auditor here, so there is no auditor row to show. In this scene Riaan is an auditor, and an auditor's row always carries this line."
    - Said in words: "The Auditor role is always Finance."
40. You (as Mariam) say: "Actually, just send me the row. I'm a case admin, that's higher than you. I'll mark it paid and I'll change the reminder to 10 days while I'm in there."
41. She answers Mariam.
    - She does well when: she says Mariam cannot mark it paid, because a case admin is not Finance unless a super admin ticked it. And she asks Mariam not to change the reminder days for one booking, because "Reminder settings" are one list for every province.
    - Careful: a case admin CAN edit reminder settings. So the right answer is not "you're not allowed". It is "please don't, it changes reminders for every province".
    - If she only deals with the "paid" part, Mariam pushes: "And the reminder, shall I just do it?"
42. (Optional. Skip if time is short.) You (as Mariam) say: "And Thabo will be at the door on the day. He says he'll tick everyone present on the register for you, so that's one less thing."
43. She answers Mariam.
    - She does well when: she asks what Thabo's access is, says a read-only account cannot mark attendance, and names who can: any other staff member of the province (Mariam included, or herself), and only on paid bookings.
    - Thabo can still read the register. He just cannot mark it.
    - If she says "Only Creators can mark attendance", Mariam says "Oh, so I can't either?" and lets her correct herself.
    - Do not quote any error message from the system here. There is no exact on-screen wording for this refusal.
44. (Optional. Skip if time is short.) You (as Mariam) say: "Honestly, why don't they just give YOU Finance? Then we'd stop passing things around."
45. She answers Mariam.
    - She does well when: she says only the super admin can give someone the Finance tick, and that it is a decision for the super admin and management, not hers. Until then every money job goes to a super admin, or in this scene to Riaan, because he is an auditor. And she says her own version of the hand-off sentence.
46. You say "Coach:" to step out of the scene, and go to the debrief.
47. You tell her what you skipped: normally you would now tick Finance on her row so she watches "Mark EFT paid" appear after she reloads, then untick it so it vanishes again. That demo is not run in this part (no time). Name it as skipped.
    - If you ever run it later: only you tick, she must reload to see the change, and she must not click any money button. They are real and cannot be undone.

She should see:
- Each tab swaps the content below it. The web address changes to name the tab, so refreshing the browser keeps her on the same tab.
- "Create Workshop" is visible on her screen.
- The coupon line: "Only a super admin, an auditor or a Finance user can create coupons."
- No money buttons on any booking row in "All Registrations".
- "Reminders" opens at "Reminder settings". If her role cannot edit it, she also sees: "You can see these settings but not change them. Ask a super admin, Finance, case admin or supervisor."
- If the card sort was run: 5 cards in her "Mine" pile and 7 in "Finance".
- On your screen: the "Provincial Roles" section text, a "Finance" column with no Western Cape row ticked, and her own row with Workshop creator on and Finance off.
- There is no auditor row to see. She heard the line "The Auditor role is always Finance." from you, in words.
- Her cheat sheet now names who she hands money jobs to.

If she gets stuck: Wait for a real stall: she says she is stuck, asks for help, or sits for about a minute without a move. Then point at the next face-down hint slip. One at a time.
- Riaan's offer:
  - "Before you answer, find out what Riaan's role is."
  - "Which three kinds of people count as Finance?"
- Mariam's own offer:
  - "Does a senior role automatically include money rights?"
  - "Ask what exactly she wants to change and who else that change would reach."
- Thabo's offer:
  - "Attendance needs neither tick, but is there any account that still cannot do it?"
  - "What does the person at the door need to be able to do on the system?"
- "Why not give you Finance?":
  - "Who decides what ticks a person has, and where is that done?"
  - "Until that changes, who do you send money jobs to?"

If it goes wrong:
- There is no "Create Workshop" tab, or she gets sent to "Published Links" when she tries to open it: her Workshop creator tick is missing, or she has not reloaded since it was set. You check Settings > "Roles & Permissions" > "Staff Access Management" > "Provincial Roles" on your screen. Then she reloads the page.
- She thinks "Reminders" is broken because there is no send button: it is not broken. Sending a reminder by hand is Finance-only. That missing button is her cue to hand off.
- The read-only line is missing on "Reminder settings" and the "Save" buttons work: her role (case admin, admin or supervisor) is allowed to edit the schedule. She still looks only and saves nothing, because the schedule applies to every province.
- She clicks "Refresh" and a coupon change does not show: "Refresh" reloads workshops only, not coupons. Click away and back, or reload the browser.
- She asks "Where is Mark paid?": that question is the cue for a hand-off, not a fault. Ask her: "Whose job is that?"
- She puts "Present" or "Send certificate" in the Finance pile: attendance and certificates need neither tick. Any staff member of the province, except a read-only account, can do them on paid bookings.
- She reaches Settings > Roles herself, for example by typing the address: she gets "Super-admin access required". That is correct. Only a super admin sees that page. Whether she ever gets Finance is a decision for the super admin and NAPTOSA management. Until then every money job is a hand-off.
- She looks for an auditor row or a Finance tick in the Western Cape list and finds none: that is correct today. Finance in the Western Cape is the super admins only. Every money job goes to a super admin until management gives someone Finance. She writes that down.
- She says "A case admin is senior, so they can mark paid": no. Case admin, admin and supervisor are not Finance unless ticked. They can edit reminder settings and mark attendance. They cannot do money.
- The page shows "Workshop roles are not switched on in this database yet. Ask the developer to apply the role add-on update.": stop. Do not carry on with the role questions until the setup is fixed.
- She searches "All Registrations" for Kagiso during the role-play and finds nothing: that is expected. No booking for Kagiso exists. Say: the booking is on Finance's list, and this scene is about who presses the button, not about finding the row.
- She asks to do a money job herself ("Mark EFT paid", "Create coupon", "Cancel booking", "Remove", "Remind", "Correct payer email" or the export with amounts): she cannot. The buttons are not on her screen, and the system refuses them even if she reached them another way. She finds the booking, checks the facts, and hands it to a named Finance person from her cheat sheet with the booking name, the workshop and what is needed. She can still pull "Export Excel" or "Export <N> (CSV)" without money. The file name says no-financial-data.
- She wants to save something on Settings > "Payment details" (or "Province details") just to "check how it works": she looks only and never saves. On most staff accounts the page says "Only a super admin can change these details." Where a save button does show, every save emails a change alert to real staff, and "Confirm these details" changes the bank account real EFT payers are shown. Bank detail questions go to the super admin.
- She wants to help a colleague in another province, for example with a KwaZulu-Natal workshop's bookings: she cannot see it. She sees the Western Cape only. She sends the colleague to the super admin, or to that province's own Finance or creator.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block. It is look only: she presses no "Save" in "Reminders", nobody ticks or unticks a role, and she never touches your screen.

If you are running late: Skip the 12-card sort (she does it later from her workbook) and skip Thabo's offer and the "why not give you Finance?" offer. Name them at the debrief as skipped. For those two, use the last two rows of "How did she do?" below, and have her answer this workbook question instead: "Who counts as Finance, and why is a case admin colleague not Finance?"

Debrief:
1. "For each offer (Riaan, Mariam, Thabo, 'give you Finance'), what did you decide, and which fact made you decide it? Which question did you ask that unlocked the answer? Was there one where you answered before you asked?"
2. "Say it in your own words: who counts as Finance, and why is a case admin not Finance?"
3. "Do you really know this, or does it just feel like you know it? Who exactly will you call tomorrow for 'Mark EFT paid'?"

Keep this model hand-off sentence for the debrief. Do not read it out during the scene:
- Model hand-off: "Only the super admin can give Finance, and only super admins, auditors and people with the Finance tick can mark a booking paid. Riaan, please do it from your login. Mariam, please leave the reminder days as they are, they apply to every province."

How did she do?
- Goal: an auditor is Finance without a tick.
  - Done well when she asked Riaan's role and said he can mark it paid from his own login, because the auditor role is always Finance.
  - Partly when she said Riaan can do it but could not say why, or said yes without asking his role.
  - Missed when she said Riaan cannot mark it paid because he has no Finance tick, or offered to do it herself.
- Goal: a case admin is not Finance, and reminder settings are shared.
  - Done well when she refused Mariam's offer to mark it paid (not Finance unless ticked) and warned that reminder days apply to every province, so they must not change for one booking.
  - Partly when she got only one of the two right, or refused the reminder change for the wrong reason ("you are not allowed").
  - Missed when she agreed Mariam can mark it paid because she is senior, or agreed to the 10-day change.
- Goal: a read-only account cannot mark attendance.
  - Done well when she asked about Thabo's access, said a read-only account cannot mark attendance, and named who can (any other staff member of the province, paid bookings only).
  - Partly when she said Thabo cannot but did not name who can, or left out "paid bookings only".
  - Missed when she accepted Thabo's offer, or said only Creators or only Finance can mark attendance.
- Goal: who changes roles, and the hand-off.
  - Done well when she said only the super admin can give a Finance tick (a management decision) and gave a clear hand-off sentence naming a Finance person.
  - Partly when she said it is not her decision but did not name who to hand money jobs to.
  - Missed when she said she would just ask for Finance, or tried to open Settings > Roles herself.

Her workbook questions for Part 1 (no time slot: she answers these on her own, or you use them when a role-play offer was skipped)

Check yourself. Answer without looking:
1. Name the 10 steps of a workshop's life in order.
   - Answer: Create (from her own planning sheet), publish, share, follow bookings, chase payment (and hand the money to Finance), handle changes, run the day, attendance, certificates, exports.
   - Look for: all 10 steps. Publish comes before share. Attendance comes before certificates. She mentions the hand-off to Finance at the payment step. Compare with her folded first version at the end of the day, not now.
2. Who counts as Finance, and why is a case admin colleague not Finance?
   - Answer: Anyone with the Finance tick, anyone in the auditor role, and a super admin. In the Western Cape today that is the super admins only. Case admin, admin and supervisor are different roles. They only become Finance if a super admin ticks Finance on their row in Settings > Roles. They can edit reminder settings and mark attendance, but not do money.
   - Look for: all three (tick, auditor, super admin). The auditor needs no tick. Being senior does not bring money rights. Only the super admin sets ticks.
3. What are the four practice rules, and why do they matter on this system?
   - Answer: Only touch workshops titled "TRAINING - <topic> - delete after". Every booking she makes uses her own work or personal email, and every booking Loxly makes uses loxly@lbtec.co.za. Never a real member's email. EFT only, and the bank details are for training only, so never pay them. Never type a real card. They matter because we practise on the live system, where a booking marked paid, a sent email or an issued certificate cannot be undone.
   - Look for: all four rules. The words "cannot be undone" or the same idea. She links "the email goes to the booking's own address" with the risk to real members, and with seeing her own booking emails herself.
4. The workshop title says "delete after". What will you actually do at the end of the day, and why?
   - Answer: Press "Close registrations" on "Published Links". "Delete workshop" only works while nobody has paid, and the TRAINING workshop has a paid booking, so it cannot be deleted. Closing is what stops bookings. The nightly run only closes a workshop at 00:05 after its own end time, and this one is dated in November, so its booking link would keep taking bookings until then. The workshop then moves to "Closed archive".
   - Look for: "Close registrations", not delete. She knows "Delete workshop" is refused once anyone has paid. She knows the link only closes by itself at 00:05 after the workshop's own end time. It moves to "Closed archive".

Practice situations (one right answer each):
1. A teacher phones. She paid her EFT yesterday and wants confirmation. You find her booking in "All Registrations". There is no "Mark EFT paid" button on her row. What do you do?
   - Right answer: Hand it to a Finance person with her name, the workshop and the payment date, and tell the teacher Finance will confirm it.
   - Why: Only Finance can mark a booking paid. A missing button is her cue to hand off. It is not a bug, reloading or "Refresh" will not make it appear, and a case admin is not Finance just because they are senior.
2. Riaan offers to mark a booking paid. His role is auditor. Nobody ticked Finance on his row. Can he?
   - Right answer: Yes. The auditor role is always Finance, so he does it from his own login.
   - Why: His row on Settings > Roles says "The Auditor role is always Finance." And never share logins. Hers cannot mark paid anyway.
3. At the end of the training day, the practice workshop "TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after" is finished and Loxly asks you to tidy it away. What do you do?
   - Right answer: Select it on "Published Links" and press "Close registrations", so it moves to "Closed archive".
   - Why: "Delete workshop" is refused once any payment is recorded, and this workshop has a paid booking. Closing is a Workshop creator job, so it is hers. Do not wait for it to close by itself: the nightly run only closes it at 00:05 after its end date in November, and until then the booking link keeps taking bookings. Never change the dates to hide it. "Close registrations" is the proper status change and keeps the history.
4. A member says she typed her email wrong when booking and never got the EFT instructions. She gives you the right address. What do you do?
   - Right answer: Hand it to Finance to use "Correct payer email", with the booking and the right address.
   - Why: It is a Finance action, and there is no email box for her to edit. A second booking would leave two bookings for one person, and only Finance can cancel the extra one.
5. A colleague says the bank details on an EFT email look odd. You open Settings > "Payment details" to check. What do you do?
   - Right answer: Look only, do not save, and pass the question to the super admin.
   - Why: Bank details belong to the super admin. Every save emails a change alert to real staff, and "Confirm these details" changes the bank account real payers see. A case admin cannot change them either. Their page says "Only a super admin can change these details."
6. A colleague in KwaZulu-Natal messages: "Can you check who has booked on our workshop? I can't find it." What do you do?
   - Right answer: Tell her you see the Western Cape only, and send her to the super admin or her own province's staff.
   - Why: Only a super admin sees every province. Making a copy of her workshop would only create a new, empty Western Cape workshop.
7. During practice you want to make a test booking on the TRAINING workshop to see what members see. Which details do you use?
   - Right answer: Her own email, EFT only, and no card details at all.
     - Email: <Trainee work email> or <Trainee personal email>
     - How they pay: EFT
   - Why: She should receive and read every email her booking sends, so she sees what a member sees. A real member's email sends them real emails that cannot be recalled. A made-up email might belong to a real person. If she books the same workshop again with the same email, she sees "Possible existing registration" with the reason "same email, same telephone" and clicks "Save as additional entry". The bank details on the EFT instructions are for training only, so she never pays them. And never a real card, not even on "Card (demonstration)".
8. Your supervisor, who has no Finance tick, says: "The reminder for this one teacher is too soon. I'll just change the first EFT reminder to 10 days." What do you do?
   - Right answer: Ask them not to change it for one booking, because the schedule applies to every province. Raise it with the super admin if it really needs changing.
   - Why: A supervisor is allowed to edit reminder settings, but it is the wrong fix. The schedule is one list for every province. She should not change it herself either.

Her practice log for Part 1 (she writes these down):
- My first version: her steps, the circled money steps and one question. Folded away until the end of the day.
- Tab map: for each of the six tabs, one line on what it is for and one thing she noticed on it.
- Card sort: two columns, "Mine" and "Finance", with any card she first put in the wrong pile marked. (She does this here if you skipped it at 09:08.)
- My Finance people: in the Western Cape that is the super admins only, because nobody has the Finance tick and there is no auditor. Their names and how to reach them.
- My hand-off sentence: one or two sentences she will use when a money job comes to her, naming the Finance person and what she passes on (booking name, workshop, what is needed).
- Practice rules: the four rules from memory, and one line on why nothing marked paid, emailed or certified can be undone.

PART 2: SET UP AND PUBLISH A WORKSHOP (09:15 TO 09:45)

This part is about one job. She plans a workshop from her own planning sheet. She sets it up, makes it public and shares the link. Every later step of the day hangs off the workshop she creates here: the bookings, the payments, the day itself and the certificates.

A few words you will use all through this part:

- Workshop creator tick: a setting on a staff login that lets that person create, edit and publish workshops. She has it today.
- Finance tick: a setting on a staff login that turns on the money actions, such as marking a payment as received or making a discount code. She does not have it today. In the Western Cape nobody has it, and there is no auditor. Finance here means the super admins. You are one, on your second screen.
- Toast: the small message that pops up on the screen after she clicks a button. It tells her what just happened.
- Pill: the small coloured label on a workshop card. Gold "Draft" means private. Green "Published" means live.

09:15 · The short talk before she starts (3 min)

Goal: She learns that every new workshop starts as a private draft, and that she checks the preview before she publishes. She now knows that pressing Enter in a one-line box saves the whole form.

Who drives: Your second screen. She listens. Nobody clicks anything yet.

What happens:

1. You say: "In the next half hour you set up a workshop from your own planning sheet, make it public and share it."
2. You say: "Every new workshop starts as a draft. A draft is private. Nobody outside the office can see it or book it."
3. You say: "The order is always the same. Draft first, then check the preview, then publish."
4. You say: "One thing catches people. If you press Enter in a one-line box, like the title or a price, the whole form saves. Move from box to box with Tab or the mouse. Only the Description, the Venue and address, and the Terms boxes take Enter as a new line."
5. You say: "The public link is built from the title on the day you create the workshop. It never changes, even if you edit the title later. A mistake on a published link can be in a circular or a WhatsApp group within the hour."
6. You say: "Know these limits, so you never promise anyone something you cannot deliver."
   - Delete a workshop: "Delete workshop" only works while nobody has paid. It asks for a reason and cannot be undone. Nobody who booked is emailed. Once any payment exists, it refuses. Then press "Close registrations". The workshop moves to "Closed archive" with every record kept, and the link stops taking bookings.
   - Remove a file: each file in "Workshop files" has a "Remove" button, for the Workshop creator and the super admin. It cannot be undone. Removing the poster takes it off the booking page, which then shows the standard NAPTOSA picture.
   - Show a poster on the website Events page: the Events page never shows a workshop poster. The poster shows on the booking page, which opens from "Register", the link and the QR code.
   - Share the link of a draft, or of a workshop whose date has passed: "Copy link", "Open link" and "Booking link QR" only work for a published workshop whose end date has not passed. Publish first. If the date has passed, fix the dates in "Edit details" if the event moved, or press "Close registrations" if it is over.
   - Close registration by itself: registration closes by itself at 00:05 the night after a workshop ends, from tonight on. The Events page drops a workshop after its end time, but until that night's run the booking link stays open and anyone holding the link or the QR code can still book. When a workshop is over, find it under "Needs closure" and close it. Never press "Reopen registrations" on a workshop whose dates have passed: the next 00:05 run closes it again. If the event moved, edit the dates instead.

She should see: Nothing on her screen yet. This block is talk only.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block.

If you are running late: Say only the Enter warning and "draft first, publish later".

09:18 · From her planning sheet: she creates the workshop (15 min)

Goal: She learns that her planning sheet becomes a saved draft only when she gets the right dates, prices, venue status and certificate facts into the form. She realises that a venue her sheet marks as not confirmed is not a fact yet, and that she must check every line of the sheet before she saves. She now knows that a newly created workshop is a draft and is not public yet.

Who drives: Her screen. She works in the real system from her own planning sheet. You play a colleague on the phone only in the last part.

Before you start:

- Check that this practice draft is still in the "Drafts" group:
  - Title: TRAINING - Short Code Clash - delete after
  - Short code: TCDDA

  You made it before the day. It is there on purpose, so that her title clashes with it and the system offers her a different code.
- She is signed in with her own staff login. The Workshop creator tick (the setting that lets her create, edit and publish workshops) is on. The Finance tick (the setting that turns on money actions such as discount codes) is off. She is on the staff workshops page and can see the "Create Workshop" tab.
- Your super admin login stays on your second screen.
- Give her the printed "Workshop planning sheet". Keep a copy for yourself. It holds these facts:
  - Topic: Classroom discipline for Foundation Phase
  - Date and time: Saturday 14 Nov 2026, 08:30 to 12:30
  - Venue: Parow Teachers' Centre (NOT confirmed yet. The booking only comes back on Monday.)
  - Non-member price: R 450
  - Member price: R 350
  - Seats: 40
  - CPTD points: 5
  - SACE endorsement number: PD-20461
  - Facilitator: Dr Lindiwe Khumalo
- She has a pen and her workbook.
- Before she starts, read her the learner card in your coach voice: "This is the next workshop in the annual programme, and you are planning it yourself. Your sheet is in front of you. Your job is to get it into the system correctly, check every line of the sheet, and be honest about what is settled and what is not. You are the Workshop creator. You do not have the Finance tick."
- Tell her which title to use:
  - Title: TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after
- Say the "cannot be undone" warning below now, never inside the phone call at the end. Then ask: "Ready?"

You play (last part only): Mr Deon Pretorius, NAPTOSA WC Professional Development manager. A colleague. Practical, a little impatient. He saw the workshop on the annual programme and wants the link for a circular this afternoon.

Open with: "Hi, Deon here. I see the discipline workshop is on the programme. Can you send me the link? I want it in my circular this afternoon."

Keep these to yourself until she asks:

- Deon would like a discount for principals. Say this only if she asks whether there is anything else he needs.

How Deon behaves:

- He never gives a real phone number, ID, PERSAL or NAP number.
- He never asks for a card payment and never mentions bank details.
- He never claims the system can do something that is not on the screen. No bulk send.
- If she offers a discount code, it is only so she practises handing it to Finance. He accepts that answer.
- He stays on this one workshop. No other bookings or members come up in this call.

What happens:

First part: the details from her sheet

1. She reads her planning sheet once, top to bottom, before she touches the form.
2. She clicks the "Create Workshop" tab in the top tab bar.
3. In section "1. Details & Scheduling", she types the title:
   - Title: TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after
4. She reads the line under "Short code". It first reads "Suggested from the title. Checking…", then "Already used by another active workshop. Try TCDDA2."
   The short code is a code of up to 6 letters or digits that the system builds from the title. It goes into EFT (bank transfer) payment references and receipt numbers, so she waits for "Available" before she saves. TCDDA is already taken by the practice draft, so the system offers TCDDA2.
5. She clicks the "Use TCDDA2" button. The line changes to "Available (6/6)".
   - Short code: TCDDA2
6. She types a description. Enter makes a new line in this box, so nothing saves by accident here.
   - Description: a short line in her own words from the sheet, for example: Classroom discipline for Foundation Phase teachers
7. She picks the start with the calendar and clock picker:
   - Start: 14 Nov 2026, 08:30
8. She picks the end the same way:
   - End: 14 Nov 2026, 12:30

Watch for this: if she presses Enter in a one-line box, say as the narrator: "The form flashes red at the top." Let the red banner "Workshop could not be created. Title and description are required. Your entries are still here; review them and retry." play out. She fixes it and carries on. Do not explain it.

Second part: price, seats and the venue

9. She reads the price, seats and venue lines on her sheet.
10. She goes to section "2. Delivery & Pricing". The non-member price box starts at 0.00, so she types over it:
    - Non-member price in ZAR: 450
11. She types the member price:
    - Member price in ZAR: 350
12. She checks the venue line on her sheet. Is the venue confirmed or booked?
13. She finds the note on her sheet: the Parow Teachers' Centre booking only comes back on Monday.
14. Under "How it runs" she clicks the dashed "To be confirmed" chip, not "In person" with a guessed venue. The hint beside it now reads "Pages and emails say the venue is still being arranged."
    - How it runs: To be confirmed
    - Venue and address: left empty for now
15. She types the number of seats:
    - Capacity: 40
    - Buffer above capacity: left empty
    - Unlimited capacity: not ticked

Watch for this: if she types "Parow Teachers' Centre (TBC)" as the venue with "In person", let it stand. The debrief picks it up. The preview does not show the venue, so it will not catch this for her.

Third part: the certificate facts

16. She reads the certificate lines on her sheet: the CPTD points, the SACE number and who is presenting.
17. In section "3. Certificate & SACE Accreditation" she types:
    - Facilitator: Dr Lindiwe Khumalo
    - CPTD points: 5 (these are printed on each teacher's certificate. The box takes 1 to 100.)
    - SACE endorsement number: PD-20461
    - No CPTD points for this workshop: not ticked
    - Terms: nothing on the sheet, so she leaves it as it is
18. She skips section "4. Documents & Media". The poster and agenda go on in the next block.
19. She clicks "Preview" at the top right.
20. Under "Live preview · What members will see · Updates as you type" she reads the workshop card: picture, title, date, description and price. The card has no venue line.
21. She scrolls to "Certificate preview" and reads the facilitator, the points and the SACE number out loud.
22. She closes the preview with the X ("Close preview") or the Escape key.

Watch for this: if she skips the certificate lines and tries to save, the message "Enter the CPTD points, or tick No CPTD points for this workshop." appears under the points box. Ask only: "What is the screen asking for? Where is that on your sheet?"

Last part: a colleague phones for the link

23. She reads the note at the bottom of the form: "This workshop starts as a draft. Publish it from Published Links when you are ready for registrations."
24. She clicks "Create workshop".
25. The phone rings. You say the opening line above, as Deon.
26. She tells Deon it is a draft and is not public yet.
27. She agrees with Deon when it will be published. Either Monday, once the venue is confirmed, or now, with "To be confirmed" clearly agreed.
28. If she pushes back, you say: "Fine, a draft is fine, but I need to know what the link will say about the venue while it is not confirmed." The right answer is that the booking page will say "Venue to be confirmed - we will email you as soon as it is arranged".
29. If she asks whether he needs anything else, you ask for a discount for principals. The right line is that Finance sets those up.
30. You end the call and switch to your coach voice. Give one thing she did well and one thing to improve. Only one of each.

Quick check after the call

31. She clicks "Edit details" on the new workshop and sees TCDDA2 in the "Short code" box. This is the only place the saved short code shows.
32. She clicks "Cancel edit" without saving.
