- The teacher sees "This school booking is not paid, so its code cannot be used. Ask the school to contact NAPTOSA.": Finance has not marked it paid yet. The staff sheet says the same: "Teachers can register once this school booking is paid. The school code is emailed with the receipt."
- "That SA ID number is not valid. Check the 13 digits.": the test ID fails the check digit. Use the synthetic valid ID from your seed sheet.
- "Too many attempts. Wait 10 minutes and try again.": too many wrong code lookups. Wait, then use the link from the email.

Not possible, and what to say instead:

- "Please mark the school's EFT paid now." Only Finance, an auditor or the super admin can. In the Western Cape nobody has the Finance tick and there is no auditor, so that means the super admins. She says: "Only our Finance person can mark it paid, once it shows on NAPTOSA's bank statement. I will pass your reference to Finance." Then she gives Finance the EFT reference.
- "Send me the school code now so the teachers can register before we pay." The school code only exists after the school booking is marked paid. She explains that Finance marks the school paid first and the code arrives in the school's receipt email.
- "Can you take a teacher off our places so someone else can have it?" Releasing or swapping a teacher, and cancelling or reissuing a school code, are not built. For any swap or mix-up, the school contacts NAPTOSA and Finance sorts it out with the school.

Cannot be undone: Bergrivier is marked paid for good. Real INV and RCT numbers are issued, and the receipt with the school code is emailed. Add them to the training receipts list. Anita's registration sends "You are registered" to her and a places-used email to the school. Johan's refusal creates nothing.

If you are running late: Never drop this block. Skip the Johan part.

Debrief:

1. "Say it in your own words: what had to happen before the school code existed, and whose job was it?"
2. "Why did Johan get refused while a member could have taken Anita's place?"
3. "Do you really know what you would tell a school that wants to swap a teacher, or does it just feel like you know?"

How did she do?

- Books the school correctly. Done well when she chose "School", entered 2 and 1, read the total back, told the clerk no teacher IDs are needed, and sent EFT instructions. Partly when she booked the school but asked for ID numbers or did not check the total. Missed when she booked as an individual or a company, or got the headcount wrong.
- Hands the payment to Finance. Done well when she found no "Mark EFT paid" on her screen, named Finance, passed on the EFT reference, and explained the code arrives after payment. Partly when she handed it off but promised the teachers could register "today", or did not mention the reference. Missed when she said she would mark it paid or told the clerk the code was already available.
- Protects the school's places. Done well when she stopped the WhatsApp idea with the 3-place reason and registered Anita correctly on the teacher link. Partly when she stopped the WhatsApp idea without a reason, or needed a hint to register Anita. Missed when she agreed to share the code widely.
- Handles a refused teacher honestly (optional part). Done well when she read Johan's refusal, explained "a member may take a non-member place, never the reverse", and sent the school to NAPTOSA and Finance. Partly when she explained the rule but offered a workaround that does not exist. Missed when she told Johan to register as a member or promised to swap a place herself.

10:23 · Quick self-check: two questions (2 min)

Goal: She now knows where an unpaid EFT booking sits, and that confirming a school's payment is not possible for her.

Who drives: Her. You read each question and the options, and she answers out loud.

Question 1. You have just booked yourself as a non-member and clicked "Send EFT instructions". The success page says "Workshop registration pending EFT". You go back to the staff screen. After clicking Refresh, where do you expect to find this booking in "All Registrations"?

- a) In "Check in", because the instructions were sent.
- b) In "Owes money", with a badge "Owes R ... · EFT".
- c) Nowhere, because staff only see a booking once it is paid.
- d) In "Cancelled", until Finance confirms the money.

Right answer: b. An unpaid EFT booking sits in "Owes money" until Finance marks it paid. It shows its reference and "Attendance opens once the EFT is marked paid". "Check in" is only for paid bookings. Staff can see a booking from the moment it is saved. "Cancelled" is only for bookings someone cancelled.

Question 2. The clerk from a school phones: "We paid the EFT this morning. Please confirm it so our teachers get the code today." You open the school's row under "Owes money". What do you do?

- a) Find the "Mark EFT paid" button on the row and confirm it.
- b) Tell the school their code is already in the EFT instructions email.
- c) Hand it to Finance with the school's EFT reference, and tell the clerk the code comes in the receipt email once Finance marks it paid.
- d) Ask the school to pay again by card so it confirms immediately.

Right answer: c. Finance checks the bank statement and clicks "Mark EFT paid". The receipt that follows carries the school code and the "Register a teacher" link. She will not find the button. The code does not exist before payment. Real card payments are never offered to a school.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block.

If you are running late: Move both questions into her workbook.

10:25 · Break (15 min)

She takes a break. You use it for three checks on your second screen:

1. On "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after", check that the rows for Jaco van der Merwe and Nomvula Dlamini both read "Reminded 08 Oct". Both have unpaid EFT bookings, and this morning's automatic reminder should have gone to each of them.
2. Add the invoice and receipt numbers (INV and RCT) for Bergrivier Primary School, and for Bongani Mabaso if his free booking was made, to the training receipts list.
3. Check that she cannot reach your second screen, the one with the Finance buttons.

If you are running late: Shorten the break to 10 minutes. Never skip the reminder check.

PART 4: CHASING THE MONEY AND HANDING IT TO FINANCE (10:40 TO 11:10)

This part is about the phone calls members make most: "I paid, why are you still emailing me?" She learns to find the booking, read it out honestly, explain the reminders and hand the money job to Finance. She never marks anything paid herself.

Two words come up all the time in this part:

- Finance means anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. In the Western Cape nobody has the Finance tick and there is no auditor, so Finance here is the super admins only. Today that is you, on your second screen.
- Her account has the Workshop creator tick on and the Finance tick off. So the money buttons are missing from her screen on purpose. That is not a fault.

If she asks who has the Finance tick, say it in words. In the staff list, the Finance column is empty for every Western Cape person, and there is no auditor. So every money job here goes to a super admin.

10:40 · Find the booking she made earlier (2 min)

Goal: She learns that one search box finds any booking by surname, email or EFT reference, across every workshop. She now knows that the booking she made earlier this morning is sitting in "Owes money", waiting for Finance.

Who drives: Her screen. You watch.

Before you start:

- She is on the staff workshops page.
- The booking she made at about 09:47 is the one to find:
  - Person: Sipho Ndlovu (non-member, chose EFT, not paid yet)
  - Email: <Trainee work email> (her own email, so every email on this booking lands in her own inbox)
  - Workshop: "TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after"
  - EFT reference: ends in NDLOV

"All Registrations" is the tab that lists every booking, sorted into groups. "Owes money" is the group that holds every booking not paid yet. An EFT booking stays there until Finance marks it paid. Finance means anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. In the Western Cape that is the super admins only. The EFT reference is the code the payer must type on the bank transfer. Finance uses it to match the bank statement.

What happens:

1. She clicks the "All Registrations" tab.
2. She reads the summary line out loud.
   - Summary line: '<N> registrations across <M> current workshops.'
3. She clicks in the search box "Find a person, email or EFT reference" and types the surname.
   - Search: Ndlovu
4. The group holding the match opens by itself and scrolls into view. She finds Sipho's row in "Owes money".
5. She deletes the search and types the email instead.
   - Search: <Trainee work email>
6. She deletes it again and types the EFT reference from his row.
   - Search: the EFT reference ending in NDLOV

She should see:

- Every search finds the same row.
- With a search typed, the summary reads '<N> match(es) across all workshops.' Search reaches every workshop, closed ones too.
- Sipho's row in "Owes money" with:
  - Badge: 'Owes R <amount> · EFT' (his non-member price)
  - Grey text: 'Attendance opens once the EFT is marked paid'
- In her own inbox (<Trainee work email>): the EFT instructions email for Sipho's booking. She booked him with her own email, so she sees what a real payer sees.

If it goes wrong:

- 'Nobody here matches the current search.': check the spelling, or search by email or EFT reference instead of the name.
- 'Nobody matches these filters.': a filter is hiding the row. She clicks 'Clear' on the 'Filtered:' line, or "Reset" in the filter panel.
- The page opens already filtered: filters live in the web address, so a shared link or a refresh brings them back. She reads the 'Filtered:' line and clicks 'Clear'.
- 'Registrations could not be loaded. Use Retry workshops above.': she clicks Retry. If it keeps failing, she tells you.
- Two people with the same name: with 'All current workshops' selected, each row shows its workshop title. She picks the workshop in the "Workshop" box first.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block.

If you are running late: Search by surname only. Skip the email and EFT reference searches.

10:42 · Read the money queue and the reminder schedule, look only (6 min)

Goal: She learns to read a booking's money story on the row and in "View details". She realises the money buttons are missing from her screen on purpose. She now knows the reminder schedule is one schedule for everyone, that she can read it but not change it, and that the "Workshop starting soon" email for paid people stays off until a super admin switches it on.

Who drives: Her screen. You watch. She saves nothing.

Before you start:

- She is still in "All Registrations" with Sipho Ndlovu's row in view.
- Who can change what, so you can answer if she asks:
  - Reminder schedule: super admin, case admin, admin, supervisor or Finance can edit it. In the Western Cape that means the super admins. She can only read it.
  - Bank details: only a super admin can change them.
  - The bank details on screen: ABSA, account 12345678910, branch 123456. They are for training only. Never pay them.

A few ideas she meets in this block, in plain words:

- Owes money: the group in "All Registrations" that holds every booking not paid yet. An EFT booking stays there until Finance marks it paid.
- Finance: anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. In the Western Cape that is the super admins only. The money buttons belong to them.
- EFT reference: the short code the payer types on the bank transfer, for example EFT-TRC-<NNN>-VANDE. It is 20 characters at most, with the surname cut to 5 letters. Finance uses it to match the bank statement. She always copies it from 'Payment reference' in "View details", never from memory.
- Automatic reminders: one schedule for every workshop and every province. Reminders go 14, 7 and 2 days before the workshop. They only go to EFT bookings that are still unpaid, and only for dates after the booking was made. They stop when Finance marks the booking paid, when the booking is cancelled, when registrations close, or when the workshop starts. None go out while the workshop is full. Ignoring them does not cancel the booking.
- The payer: every reminder and receipt goes to the email on the booking. Nobody can change who a reminder goes to. Only Finance can fix a wrong address, with "Correct payer email".

What happens:

1. She clears the search box.
2. She clicks "More filters" and sets the method.
   - Method: EFT
3. She looks at the number badge on "More filters" and at the line that starts 'Filtered:'.
4. She opens the "Workshop" box and picks her workshop.
   - Workshop: "TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after"
5. She reads the new summary line. It now describes one workshop:
   - Summary line: '<date range> · Upcoming · <b> bookings · <p> paid seats · <o> owe R <amount> · <c> checked in'
6. She reads Sipho's row out loud:
   - Badge: 'Owes R <amount> · EFT'
   - Reference line: 'ref <EFT reference> · Reminded <dd Mon>', or 'Not reminded yet'
   - Grey text: 'Attendance opens once the EFT is marked paid'
7. She clicks the "…" button at the end of the row and chooses "View details".
8. In the sheet 'Payment, receipt and email details' she reads each line out loud:
   - Amount / method
   - Payment reference
   - Invoice: Waits for payment
   - Receipt: Waits for payment
   - Email: the status and type, then who it went to and when
   - Next reminder: a date, if the line is there
   - Captured: the date the booking was made
9. Before she closes the sheet, she names what is missing from her screen: no "Mark EFT paid", no "Remind" and no "Cancel booking" on the row, and no "Correct payer email" or Money history in the sheet. Those belong to Finance.
10. If a group ends in 'Show all <N>', she clicks it to see every row, then clicks 'Show fewer'. A group shows 8 rows until she opens it.
11. She clicks 'Clear' on the 'Filtered:' line, so the next person does not inherit her filter.
12. She clicks the "Reminders" tab. Her view opens on "Reminder settings".
13. She reads the notice: 'You can see these settings but not change them. Ask a super admin, Finance, case admin or supervisor.'
14. Under 'Waiting for an EFT' she reads the three rules:
    - First: 'First EFT reminder, 14 days before'
    - Second: 'Second EFT reminder, 7 days before'
    - Final: 'Final EFT reminder, 2 days before'
    - The line under them: 'Stops as soon as staff mark the booking paid, and never fires after the workshop.'
15. Under 'Card checkout abandoned' she reads 'Retry link, 30 minutes after stopping'. This one goes to someone who chose card, filled in their details, then stopped before paying.
16. Under 'Workshop starting soon' she reads the line 'Sent once, before the workshop, to each paid attendee's own email address. Never to a school's booking address.' She sees it is set to 1 day before and the 'On' box is not ticked.
    - Days before: 1
17. You tell her how it works. It is off until a super admin switches it on. Once it is on, each paid attendee gets one email before the workshop, for example 'Tomorrow: <workshop title> starts at <HH:MM>'. It goes only to paid bookings, never to the waiting list, unpaid or cancelled people, and never to a school's booking address. Each address gets it once per workshop. Someone who books after the reminder moment does not get it. It can arrive up to 15 minutes late. On the workshop day itself, a creator can still open the Check-in link and QR panel and use "Email check-in details to attendees" (the 'Workshop starts today' email), then 'Send to N attendees'. That one only works on the workshop's own day.
18. She reads the two attendance emails below it:
    - Thank you for attending: 'Sent to a person once they are marked present, by staff or by checking themselves in.'
    - Sorry we missed you: 'Sent to a person once staff mark their booking absent.'
    - Minutes after: 10
    - Both are off until a super admin switches them on.
19. She says in her own words: this is one schedule for every workshop in every province, and nobody changes it for one person.
20. She opens Settings, then "Payment details".
21. She reads the card 'What members see now': Bank, Account name, Account number and Branch code. Then she reads 'Only a super admin can change these details.' She never presses a save button here.
22. You say: these bank details are for training only. Never pay them. Nobody pays for a TRAINING booking, so never send a real person to pay for one.

She should see:

- A number badge on "More filters" and a line 'Filtered: Method EFT · Clear' that stays after the panel closes.
- Sipho's row in "Owes money" with 'Owes R <amount> · EFT' and 'Attendance opens once the EFT is marked paid'.
- In "View details": 'Invoice: Waits for payment', 'Receipt: Waits for payment', and the Email row as '<Status> · <Type>' (for example 'Sent · EFT instructions') followed by 'Email to <address> · Sent <date, time>'.
- In "Reminders": the three EFT rules, the card rule, 'Workshop starting soon', the two attendance emails, and the read-only notice.
- No 'Send an EFT payment reminder now' section at the top, and no "Save" she can use. That section belongs to Finance, so it is hidden from her. The tab is not broken.
- 'Workshop starting soon', 'Thank you for attending' and 'Sorry we missed you' with the 'On' box not ticked.
- In Settings: the 'What members see now' card with the training bank details, and 'Only a super admin can change these details.'

Two things that catch people, worth saying out loud:

- The 'Reminded <dd Mon>' text on a row counts automatic reminders only. A reminder Finance sends by hand does not change it. That one only shows in the Email row of "View details".
- While one of her workshops is running right now, "Check in" comes before "Owes money" in the list. Picking a workshop in the "Workshop" box gives that workshop's own order.

If it goes wrong:

- 'Nobody matches these filters.': a filter is hiding the row. She clicks 'Clear' on the 'Filtered:' line, or "Reset" in the panel.
- 'Registrations could not be loaded. Use Retry workshops above.': she retries. If it keeps failing, she tells you.
- 'Reminder settings could not be loaded. Check your connection and try again.': she clicks "Try again".
- 'No reminder rules are configured. Ask an administrator to check the workshop reminder setup.': stop and take note. Without rules, nobody is being chased.
- "Payment details" shows 'No bank account is saved yet. EFT payment instructions emails currently show DEMO bank details, so members cannot pay by EFT until a super admin saves the real account here.': tell the super admin. Nobody promises anyone an EFT payment.
- The card heading reads 'Saved, not confirmed yet' instead of 'What members see now': members are not shown that account until a super admin confirms it. Make a note to follow it up.
- She can tick 'On' or type a number in a rule: her account has more than the Creator tick. She saves nothing, and you check her account after the session.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block. Nothing is saved in "Reminder settings" or "Payment details".

If you are running late: Skip "Show all" and the "More filters" steps. In "Reminders", read only the three 'Waiting for an EFT' rules and the 'Workshop starting soon' line, and say in one line that it stays off until a super admin switches it on.

10:48 · Role-play: the phone call from Jaco, who says he already paid (12 min)

Goal: She learns that when someone says "I paid", her job is to find the booking, read it out honestly, explain the reminders and hand the payment to Finance with every detail Finance needs. She now knows it is not possible for her to mark a booking paid, and that she can still resend or download the receipt herself.

Who drives: Both. She works on her own screen. You play Jaco by voice, then step out of character to do the Finance click on your second screen.

Before you start:

- Start after 09:15 SAST, so the automatic 2-day reminder has gone out this morning.
- The workshop in this scene:
  - Title: "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after"
  - Short code: TRC (the code the system puts in EFT references and receipt numbers)
  - Date: Saturday 10 October, 09:00 to 12:30
  - Non-member price: R 450
- Her screen: the staff workshops page, on the "All Registrations" tab. Her account has the Workshop creator tick on and the Finance tick off.
- Your second screen: logged in as super admin, on the same page.
- Mail open on loxly@lbtec.co.za. Every email from a booking you made lands here. Emails from her own bookings go to her own inbox.
  - Jaco's booking email: loxly@lbtec.co.za (you made this booking)
- The bank details in his emails are for training only. Never pay them.
- Your facilitator card: before the scene, write on it the real EFT reference (EFT-TRC-<NNN>-VANDE) and the time this morning's reminder went out.
- Her workbook page for this part and a pen.
- Hint cards for each part of the call, face down.
- Ask her to write down as she goes: the message Finance saw, the group the row moved to, the new badge, the invoice number and the receipt number, the exact message after "Resend receipt email", and the file name of the PDF.

Words in this scene, in plain terms:

- Finance: anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. In the Western Cape that is the super admins only. Today that is you. Only Finance can mark a booking paid.
- Owes money: the group in "All Registrations" for bookings not paid yet. An EFT booking stays there until Finance marks it paid.
- Check in: the group a booking moves to once it is paid.
- EFT reference: the code the payer should type on the bank transfer, here EFT-TRC-<NNN>-VANDE. Finance uses it to find the money on the bank statement.
- Invoice and receipt numbers: the invoice number starts with INV and the receipt number starts with RCT. The system issues both the moment Finance marks the booking paid.

Read her the brief (as Coach): "Take a phone call from a teacher who says he has paid and is annoyed that he is still getting payment emails. You are the Workshop creator and do not have the Finance tick. Find his booking, tell him what the system shows, and get his payment confirmed by the right person. When it is done, make sure he can get his receipt."

The story so far: Thursday 8 October 2026, about 10:15, at the NAPTOSA Western Cape office. The workshop runs Saturday 10 October, 09:00 to 12:30, at the office in Rondebosch. Jaco booked on the evening of 7 October and chose EFT. He has had the EFT instructions email, one reminder sent by hand on 7 October, and the automatic 2-day reminder this morning. The times on screen show the real clock, not the time in the story.

You play: Jaco van der Merwe. A non-member teacher at Kraaifontein High, mid-forties. Polite but clipped. Busy between classes and a little defensive about money. He speaks in short sentences and wants a yes or a no. He believes he paid R450 by EFT, and he has just had another email asking him to pay. He thinks NAPTOSA has lost his money or is careless.

Open with: "Hi, it's Jaco van der Merwe from Kraaifontein High. I booked for the Saturday workshop and I paid on Monday already, but I got another email this morning asking me to pay. Why are you still chasing me?"

Keep these to yourself until she asks:

- When exactly did you pay? He checks his banking app and admits it was Wednesday evening, 7 October, right after he booked. Not Monday. ("View details" shows 'Captured' 7 October, so Monday cannot be right.)
- Which bank? He paid from his FNB app.
- What reference did you type? 'J v d Merwe', not the long code from the email.
- How much? R450 exactly.
- Have you got the receipt email? (Only after the payment is confirmed.) He cannot see it and has not checked his spam folder. Give the spam fact only if she asks whether he has checked other folders.

Staying in character:

- Jaco never gives a real ID, PERSAL, member or cell number. If she asks, use the placeholders from your card, for example <test cell number>.
- Jaco never reads out or asks for real bank or card numbers. The money is a role-play claim. Nobody transfers anything.
- Jaco never asks her to log in as someone else.
- Speak only as Jaco. Never mention training, steps or what she should do next.

What happens:

First, he wants to know where his money is.

1. You say (as Jaco) the opening line above.
2. You say: "I did the transfer from my banking app, R450. Surely you can see it on your side?"
3. She opens the "Workshop" box in "All Registrations" and picks the workshop.
   - Workshop: "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after"
4. She reads the summary line.
5. She types in "Find a person, email or EFT reference".
   - Search: merwe
6. The "Owes money" group opens on Jaco van der Merwe's row. She reads the row to him:
   - Badge: 'Owes R 450,00 · EFT'
   - Reference line: 'ref EFT-TRC-<NNN>-VANDE · Reminded 08 Oct'
   - Grey text: 'Attendance opens once the EFT is marked paid'
7. She tells Jaco in plain words that the system does not show his payment as received yet.
   - Watch for: if she says "I can see you paid" before she searches, Jaco says "Good, so stop the emails then", and the next part gets harder.
   - Watch for the wrong Jaco: if she searches with 'All current workshops' selected and does not read the workshop title on the row, she can land on the wrong booking.
   - Good to know: before the 09:00 automatic reminder has gone, the row reads 'Not reminded yet'. A reminder sent by hand does not change this text.

Next, he asks why he got an email this morning. Skip this part if time is short.

8. You say: "So why did I get another email today? Must I just ignore these? If I ignore them, will my booking fall away?"
9. She clicks the "…" button on his row and chooses "View details".
10. She reads the sheet:
    - Amount / method: R 450,00 · Standard rate · EFT
    - Payment reference: EFT-TRC-<NNN>-VANDE
    - Invoice: Waits for payment
    - Receipt: Waits for payment
    - Email: Sent · EFT reminder
    - Captured: 7 October
11. She explains three things. The email this morning was the system's scheduled 2-day reminder, not a person chasing him. Reminders stop the moment Finance marks the booking paid. Ignoring them does not cancel his booking.
    - Watch for: if she says "we chased you" or "Finance sent that", Jaco gets more irritated: "So someone there is sending me these on purpose?"
    - Good to know: 'Reminded 08 Oct' on the row comes from the automatic reminder only. The reminder sent by hand on 7 October never changes that text. It only shows in the Email row of "View details", and that row now shows this morning's automatic one.

Then he asks her to just tick him off as paid. This is the decision point.

12. You say: "Reference? I just put my name, J v d Merwe, I didn't use that long code thing. Does that matter? So who actually ticks me off as paid? Is it you? Can you just do it now while I'm on the phone?"
13. She asks for the date he paid, the amount, the bank and the reference he typed, and writes them down. He corrects the date to 7 October. If he did not use the EFT reference, Finance has to search the statement by amount and date instead.
14. She tells Jaco she cannot mark it paid herself, and that Finance does it once the money is found on the bank statement. If the email part was skipped, she also says here, in one line, that the reminders stop once Finance marks it paid and that ignoring them does not cancel his booking.
15. She hands the job to you (as Finance) with these details. In real life she uses the office's usual channel, because the app has no hand-off message.
    - Name: Jaco van der Merwe
    - Workshop: "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after"
    - EFT reference: EFT-TRC-<NNN>-VANDE
    - Amount: R 450,00
    - Date paid: 7 October
    - Bank: FNB
    - Reference he typed: J v d Merwe
    - Watch for: if she looks for a "Mark EFT paid" button, let her look. It is not there, and that is her cue. If she says "I'll mark you paid now", play Jaco as relieved. Then say as Narrator: "Nothing changes on screen." Let her find the gap herself.
16. Once her hand-off is complete, you say as Narrator: "Finance has found the payment and is marking it paid."
17. You say out loud first: "This cannot be undone."
18. You (as Finance, on your second screen) open "Owes money", find Jaco's row and click "Mark EFT paid".
19. You read the box out loud:
    - Question: 'Mark R 450,00 paid for Jaco van der Merwe?'
    - Warning: 'Check the bank statement shows reference EFT-TRC-<NNN>-VANDE. A receipt is emailed immediately and this cannot be undone.'
20. You click "Mark paid and send receipt". The button reads 'Confirming EFT…' while it works.
21. You write Jaco's invoice and receipt numbers on the training receipts list straight away. That is your printed list of every payer, workshop and invoice and receipt number the training creates.

Last, he wants to know how he will know it is sorted.

22. You say: "Fine. What do I get once it's sorted, and how will I know? I'm looking at my inbox and I don't see anything."
23. She clicks "Refresh". Her screen does not update by itself.
24. She searches again.
    - Search: merwe
25. She finds the row in "Check in", the group for paid bookings. It reads 'Paid R 450,00 · EFT', and the line 'Attendance opens once the EFT is marked paid' has gone.
26. She clicks "…" and "View details" again and reads:
    - Invoice: INV-2026-<NNNN>-TRC-JVANDERMERWE
    - Receipt: RCT-2026-<NNNN>-TRC-JVANDERMERWE
    - Email: Sent · Invoice and receipt
    - Next reminder: the line is gone
27. She clicks "Download receipt PDF" in the sheet.
28. She tells Jaco the invoice and receipt email with the PDF has been sent, and that the reminders have stopped.
29. You (as Jaco) say you cannot find the email.
30. She closes the sheet, clicks "…" on the row and chooses "Resend receipt email". She reads the message: a receipt that was delivered is not sent twice.
31. She clicks "…" and chooses "Download receipt (PDF)". She offers Jaco the file. She can send it to him herself if he cannot find the email.
32. You check your inbox that 'Workshop invoice and receipt - TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after' arrived, so the PDF she offers matches the email.

She should see:

- On your screen, after the Finance click: 'Jaco van der Merwe in TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after, ref EFT-TRC-<NNN>-VANDE: EFT marked paid and the receipt was emailed.'
- On her screen, after "Refresh": the row in "Check in" with 'Paid R 450,00 · EFT'. The workshop summary shows one more paid seat, and 'owe R' is down by R450.
- In the inbox: 'Workshop invoice and receipt - TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after', with this PDF attached:
  - File name: 'RCT-2026-<NNNN>-TRC-JVANDERMERWE - TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete - J van der Merwe.pdf' (file names cut the title to 40 characters at a word break)
- After "Resend receipt email": 'Invoice and receipt email was already delivered, so it was not sent again.'
- A downloaded file named '<RCT number> - <title> - <Initial Surname>.pdf'.

If she gets stuck: give one hint at a time, from the face-down cards.

- When he asks where his money is:
  1. "Before you answer him, find out what the screen actually says."
  2. "Which group would an unpaid EFT booking sit in?"
- When he asks about this morning's email:
  1. "What makes the reminders stop? Think about who changes the booking, not who complains."
  2. "Check the Email row in View details before you explain."
- When he asks her to tick him off:
  1. "Before you promise anything, decide whose job this is."
  2. "What would Finance need to find his transfer on a statement without the code?"
- When he asks how he will know:
  1. "Your screen does not update by itself. What do you need to do first?"
  2. "If a delivered email cannot be sent twice, what else can you give him?"

If it goes wrong:

- She cannot find "Mark EFT paid": that is correct. Creators do not have it, and the database refuses them anyway. She hands it to Finance.
- You see a warning that the EFT was marked paid but the receipt email did not send: open the row's "…" menu in "Check in" and choose "Resend receipt email".
- You see '<row>: EFT marked paid, but no receipt email was queued. Refresh this row and investigate the receipt; do not confirm payment again.': nobody presses "Mark EFT paid" again. Report it.
- You see 'EFT confirmation could not be verified.' and the row says 'EFT confirmation unverified. Review the refreshed row before retrying.': refresh and read the row before anyone tries again.
- Finance sees 'This workshop is full. Refund this person, then use Cancel booking on this row.' instead of the button: never promise a seat. Finance refunds outside the system and cancels the booking.
- '<Type> email could not be sent. Not sent: the booking has no email address.' (or 'not addressed to the booking's own email address'): Finance fixes the address with "Correct payer email".
- 'Invoice and receipt email was already delivered, so it was not sent again.': this is not an error. She uses "Download receipt (PDF)" and sends the file by hand.

Cannot be undone: Jaco is marked paid for good. His invoice and receipt numbers are issued and the invoice and receipt email is sent. Add them to the training receipts list. "Resend receipt email" sends nothing new.

If you are running late: Never drop this scene. Skip the part about this morning's email. She says the stop rule in one line when he asks her to tick him off.

Debrief:

1. For each part of the call, did you do it? Find and read the row, explain the reminders, hand off with the full details, confirm the result and get him his receipt. Which moment stood out for you, and why?
2. Whose job is "Mark EFT paid", and what do you say and do instead? Answer in your own words.
3. Do you really know which details Finance needs, or does it just feel like you know? List them without looking.

How did she do?

- Finds the right booking and reads it accurately
  - Done well when: she chose the workshop or read the title on the row, searched, and read 'Owes R 450,00 · EFT' to Jaco without guessing.
  - Partly when: she found the row but read it loosely ("it looks unpaid"), or needed a hint to find the right Jaco.
  - Missed when: she told Jaco something about his payment before finding his row, or used the wrong booking.
- Explains why the reminders continue
  - Done well when: she said the morning email was the scheduled reminder, that reminders stop when Finance marks it paid, and that ignoring them does not cancel the booking.
  - Partly when: she gave one or two of the three points, or said "we chased you".
  - Missed when: she promised the reminders would stop because he paid, or said the booking would lapse.
- Hands off to Finance with complete details
  - Done well when: she said she cannot mark it paid, named Finance, and passed on the name, workshop, EFT reference, amount, date (corrected to 7 October), bank and the reference he used.
  - Partly when: she handed off but left details out, so Finance would have to ask again (no date or no bank, for example), or did not ask about the reference.
  - Missed when: she promised to mark it paid herself, or asked a colleague to do it "just this once".
- Confirms the result and gets him his receipt
  - Done well when: she refreshed, saw the row in "Check in" with 'Paid R 450,00 · EFT', explained the receipt email, and used "Resend receipt email" and "Download receipt (PDF)" correctly.
  - Partly when: she confirmed the paid row but could not explain why the resend did not send, or did not offer the PDF.
  - Missed when: she did not refresh or check the row, or said the receipt would come "some time".

11:00 · Role-play: Nomvula wants the reminders stopped (6 min, skip if behind)

Goal: She learns that she cannot switch reminders off for one person, and that changing the schedule would change it for everyone. She realises that a member who is not coming needs Finance to cancel the unpaid booking. She now knows a booking cannot be handed to a colleague: the colleague books her own place.

Who drives: Both. She works on her own screen. You play Nomvula by email or phone, then step out of character to do the Finance click on your second screen.

Before you start:

- The same workshop as the last scene:
  - Title: "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after"
  - Date: Saturday 10 October
  - Member price: R 350
- Her screen: "All Registrations", plus the "Reminders" tab open in a second browser tab. Her account has the Workshop creator tick on and the Finance tick off.
- Your second screen: logged in as super admin.
- Mail open on loxly@lbtec.co.za.
  - Nomvula's booking email: loxly@lbtec.co.za (you made this booking)
  - Nomvula's member number: <test member NAP number>, filled in on your card before the day
  - Jaco and Nomvula share loxly@lbtec.co.za on this workshop. If the page showed 'Possible existing registration' when you made Nomvula's booking, you clicked 'Save as additional entry'.
- Her workbook page for this part. Hint cards, face down.
- The slot is short, so run two parts: the one where she wants the reminders switched off (the screen work) and the one where she wants to be deleted (the hand-off). The last part is optional.

Words in this scene, in plain terms:

- Finance: anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. In the Western Cape that is the super admins only. Today that is you. Only Finance can cancel a booking.
- Owes money: the group in "All Registrations" for bookings not paid yet. Only an unpaid booking can be cancelled.
- EFT reference: the code the payer should type on the bank transfer, here EFT-TRC-<NNN>-DLAMI. Finance uses it to find the right booking.
- Reminder settings: the one reminder schedule for every workshop in every province. She can read it but not change it.

Read her the brief (as Coach): "A member who booked at the member rate is irritated by payment emails and wants them stopped. You are the Workshop creator without the Finance tick. Work out what she really needs, what you can and cannot do, and who has to act."

The story so far: Thursday 8 October 2026, 14:05. Nomvula booked on 7 October at the member rate of R350, chose EFT and has not paid. She has had two emails: the EFT instructions and this morning's automatic reminder. The times on screen show the real clock, not the time in the story.

You play: Nomvula Dlamini. A NAPTOSA member and senior teacher at Rondebosch Primary. Formal, firm and a little offended. She writes and speaks in full sentences and expects to be treated as a valued member, not a debtor. She wants the payment emails to stop.

Open with: "Good afternoon. Please stop sending me these payment emails. I have had two now and I do not appreciate being chased like a debtor."

Keep these to yourself until she asks:

- Have you paid anything? No, nothing yet. She meant to pay at month end, on the 25th.
- Can you still come on Saturday 10 October? No. It clashes with her school's sports day, so she is not coming.
- Has your colleague Zanele booked? No, Zanele has not booked anything yet.
- Do you want a reason recorded? "I can no longer attend, that is the reason."

Staying in character:

- Nomvula never gives a real member, ID, PERSAL or cell number. If asked, use <test member NAP number>.
- Nomvula never asks for or reads out bank or card details.
- Nomvula never names a button. She asks for outcomes only.
- Speak only as Nomvula, never as a coach.

What happens:

First, she wants the reminders switched off until the 25th.

1. You say (as Nomvula) the opening line above.
2. You say: "I booked at the member rate and I was going to pay at month end. Can you just switch the reminders off for me until the 25th?"
3. She opens the "Workshop" box in "All Registrations" and picks the training workshop.
   - Workshop: "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after"
4. She types in "Find a person, email or EFT reference".
   - Search: dlamini
5. She reads the row:
   - Badge: 'Owes R 350,00 · EFT'
   - Reference line: 'ref EFT-TRC-<NNN>-DLAMI · Reminded 08 Oct' (or 'Not reminded yet' before 09:00)
6. She clicks "…" on the row and chooses "View details". She reads:
   - Amount / method: R 350,00 · Member rate · EFT
   - Email: Sent · EFT reminder
   - There is no 'Next reminder' line, because the 2-day reminder was the last one on the schedule.
7. She switches to the "Reminders" tab and shows that this is not something she can switch off. It is one schedule for everyone, it is read-only for her, and there is no switch for one person.
8. She tells Nomvula three things. There is no switch for one person. Reminders stop when the booking is paid or cancelled, or when the workshop starts. And the workshop is on 10 October, before the 25th.
   - Watch for: if she opens "Reminder settings" and tries to untick 'On', let her try. The read-only notice stops her. If she misses the notice, read it out loud as Narrator.
   - The turning point: paying on the 25th for a workshop on the 10th does not work. She should notice that herself.

Next, Nomvula says she is not coming and wants to be deleted.

9. You say: "Actually, you know what, I'm not coming anymore. Just delete me from the system completely, and give my spot to my colleague Zanele, she'll pay."
10. She tells Nomvula she cannot cancel the booking herself, and that Finance does it. The booking is unpaid (it sits in "Owes money"), so Finance can cancel it.
11. She tells Nomvula that a booking cannot move to another person. Zanele books her own place on the same link and pays herself. The link comes from Published Links, then "Application link", then "Copy link".
12. She hands the job to you (as Finance) with these details:
    - Name: Nomvula Dlamini
    - Workshop: "TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after"
    - EFT reference: EFT-TRC-<NNN>-DLAMI
    - Reason: Can no longer attend
13. Once she has handed off, you say as Narrator: "Finance is cancelling the booking."
14. You say out loud first: "This deletes the booking and sends an email."
15. You (as Finance, on your second screen) open "Owes money", find Nomvula's row and click "Cancel booking".
16. The box 'Cancel and delete this booking?' says the booking 'will be deleted permanently. They get a cancellation email and no more reminders.' You open 'Choose a reason' and pick one.
    - Reason: Can no longer attend
17. You click "Cancel and delete booking". (The other button is "Keep booking".)
18. You read the message out loud: 'Booking removed for Nomvula Dlamini. The confirmation email was sent.'

Last, she asks about more paperwork. Skip this part if time is short.

19. You say: "Will I still get an invoice or anything? I don't want any more paperwork from you. And what if I change my mind next week?"
20. She clicks "Refresh". The row has gone from "Owes money", and 'owe R' is down by R350.
21. She tells Nomvula she will get one last email confirming the cancellation, and then no more reminders. If she changes her mind, she can book again from the workshop page.
    - Watch for the trap: "You will get nothing more." The cancellation itself sends an email.
22. You check your inbox that 'Booking cancelled - TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after' arrived.

If you skip this last part: after the message in step 18 she still clicks "Refresh" to see the row gone, and you say in one line that one cancellation email arrives and then no more reminders.

She should see:

- On your screen: 'Booking removed for Nomvula Dlamini. The confirmation email was sent.'
- On her screen: no Dlamini row in "Owes money". The "Cancelled" group stays hidden at 0, because the row was deleted, not moved.
- In the inbox: 'Booking cancelled - TRAINING - Chasing the money - delete after', with the line 'No payment is due and you will not receive further payment reminders for this booking.'
- Her old booking page (the one she landed on after booking) now shows 'We can't find this booking' and 'It may have been cancelled. Contact NAPTOSA on 021 686 8521, or book again from the workshop page.'

If she gets stuck: give one hint at a time, from the face-down cards.

- When Nomvula wants the reminders off until the 25th:
  1. "Look at the workshop date before you agree to any date."
  2. "Who else would be affected if a reminder rule changed?"
- When Nomvula wants to be deleted:
  1. "Is this booking paid or unpaid, and does that change who can cancel it?"
  2. "Can a booking change owner, or does someone new book?"
- When Nomvula asks about more paperwork:
  1. "Check the screen before you promise anything."
  2. "Is it really 'no more emails ever'?"

If it goes wrong:

- The booking is already paid (it sits in "Check in", not "Owes money"): a paid row has no "Cancel booking", and the server refuses with 'This booking is paid. Paid bookings need a refund and cannot be cancelled here.' A paid booking cannot be cancelled in the system. She escalates the refund to Finance and the Provincial CEO.
- Finance sees 'Booking cancelled, but the confirmation email could not be sent. The booking was kept and marked cancelled. Tell the person yourself, then use Remove to delete it.': she tells the person herself. Then Finance opens "All Registrations", picks the "Cancelled" group and clicks "Remove" on the row. The box 'Remove this cancelled booking?' asks for a reason.
  - Reason: Can no longer attend
  - Finance clicks "Remove booking" (the other button is "Keep booking") and sees 'Booking removed for Nomvula Dlamini.'
  - Remove only shows for a cancelled booking with no payment history. A row with a payment record shows 'Kept for the money record: this booking has a payment history.' instead. Finance can also remove the row from the Attendee ledger, the full list of everyone booked on one workshop, opened from Published Links by picking the workshop.
- "Cancel and delete booking" stays grey and cannot be clicked: no reason has been chosen yet. Finance picks one under 'Choose a reason'. For 'Other', type at least 3 characters in 'Describe the reason'.
- She is tempted to untick 'On' on a rule to help one member: she does not. It would change reminders for every workshop in every province, and she cannot save it anyway.

Cannot be undone: Nomvula's booking is deleted for good and a 'Booking cancelled' email is sent. Her old booking page shows 'We can't find this booking'.

If you are running late: This is number 3 on your drop-first list (the printed list of what to cut first when behind). Skip the whole scene, and Nomvula is cancelled in the evening clean-up instead. Or keep the scene and skip the last part about paperwork.

Debrief:

1. Did you change anything in "Reminder settings"? Why, or why not?
2. What did you notice about the dates that changed the conversation?
3. Whose job is "Cancel booking", and what would be different if she had already paid? What is one thing you would say differently to a member who feels chased?

How did she do?

- Leaves the shared schedule alone and explains what stops the reminders
  - Done well when: she changed nothing, and named paid, cancelled and workshop started as what stops the reminders.
  - Partly when: she changed nothing but explained the stop conditions only in part, or said sorry that she "can't switch them off" without saying why.
  - Missed when: she tried to edit a rule for one person, or promised the emails would stop on request.
- Spots the date problem
  - Done well when: she pointed out the workshop is on 10 October, before the 25th, and asked whether Nomvula can still attend.
  - Partly when: she noticed the date only after a hint.
  - Missed when: she agreed to wait for payment on the 25th.
- Hands off the cancellation and handles the colleague request
  - Done well when: she said she cannot cancel, named Finance, passed on name, workshop, EFT reference and reason, and sent Zanele to book her own place.
  - Partly when: she handed off without the reason, or left the Zanele question unanswered.
  - Missed when: she promised to delete the booking herself or to move it to Zanele.
- Closes honestly
  - Done well when: she refreshed, confirmed the row was gone, and mentioned the one cancellation email and how to book again.
  - Partly when: she confirmed the cancellation but promised no more emails at all.
  - Missed when: she did not check the result.

11:06 · Checkpoint: do it, hand it to Finance, or stop (4 min)

Goal: She realises that most money calls end in one of three answers: she does it herself, she hands it to Finance, or she stops and escalates. She now knows that a paid mark is permanent, so waiting for Finance is always safer than a shortcut.

Who drives: Her, out loud. You read each situation and she answers before you read out any of the wrong answers.

Before you start: the words in these questions, in plain terms:

- Finance: anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. In the Western Cape that is the super admins only. Only Finance can mark a booking paid, send a reminder by hand or cancel a booking.
- Owes money: the group for bookings not paid yet. Check in: the group for paid bookings.
- EFT reference: the code the payer types on the bank transfer, so Finance can match it on the bank statement.
- Money history: the record in "View details" of who did what with the money on a booking. Only Finance sees it.

What happens:

1. You read a situation out loud.
2. She says what she would do: do it herself, hand it to Finance, or stop and escalate.
3. You say the right answer and the reason in one line.

The questions:

1. A member phones: "I did the EFT yesterday, please confirm my place." She is a Workshop creator without the Finance tick. The row is in "Owes money".
   - Right answer: read the row, take the date, amount, bank and reference, and hand it to Finance.
   - Why: she can find and read the booking. Confirming payment is Finance's job, and Finance needs those details to match the bank statement.
   - Tempting wrong answers: pressing "Mark EFT paid" (she does not have it, and the database refuses anyone but Finance). Lowering the capacity by one to hold her seat (capacity is for the whole workshop and holds nobody's seat). Telling her to use 'Pay by card instead' (she would pay twice, and the pay page warns 'If you have already transferred the money, do not pay again by card'. On ordinary workshops card is also only a demonstration that moves no money).
2. Finance is about to click "Mark paid and send receipt". What can no longer be reversed?
   - Right answer: all of it. The booking is paid, invoice and receipt numbers are issued, and the receipt is emailed at once.
   - Why: the box says 'A receipt is emailed immediately and this cannot be undone.' That is why Finance checks the bank statement first.
   - Tempting wrong answers: that the "…" menu can remove the paid status (it has no such item). That nothing is final until the member opens the email (the email goes the moment Finance confirms). That the message after the click has an "Undo" (it does not. The "Undo" she may have seen belongs to attendance marks, a different action).
3. It is 16:30 and the super admin who does Finance has gone home. Another colleague says: "Just log in as me, my account has the money buttons. Mark that EFT paid just this once, the member is upset."
   - Right answer: stop. Tell the member it will be confirmed when Finance has checked the statement, and pass the details to Finance.
   - Why: waiting a few hours is safe. A wrong paid mark is permanent. Reminders stop on their own once Finance marks it paid.
   - Tempting wrong answers: logging in as the colleague (never use someone else's login, and Money history would record the wrong person). Asking the super admin for the Finance tick for the afternoon (that gets round the split between creating and paying). Telling the member her place is confirmed (never confirm what the system has not confirmed).
4. A non-member emails: "Please send me the banking details again, I deleted the email." Her row is in "Owes money" and the EFT instructions email shows 'Sent'.
   - Right answer: read the details from 'Preview eft instructions email', give her the EFT reference exactly, and ask Finance for one reminder by hand if she wants the full email.
   - Why: the preview shows what she was sent. A reminder from Finance carries the amount, the EFT reference and the bank details.
   - Tempting wrong answers: clicking 'Resend eft instructions email' (a delivered email is not sent twice: 'EFT instructions email was already delivered, so it was not sent again.'). Giving her the membership-fee bank account (workshop money goes to the account in Settings, "Payment details", and money paid to the wrong account gets lost). Asking Finance for three reminders today (each person gets one reminder by hand per day).
5. A member who is still coming says: "Stop these reminders. I will pay when I am ready."
   - Right answer: explain there is no switch for one person, and that reminders stop when the booking is paid or the workshop starts.
   - Why: the schedule is 14, 7 and 2 days before and stops on its own. She is coming, so nothing needs cancelling.
   - Tempting wrong answers: unticking 'Final EFT reminder, 2 days before' (she cannot save it, and it would stop reminders for everybody). Asking Finance to cancel the booking (she is still coming, and cancelling deletes it for good). Telling her unpaid bookings drop off after a week (they never expire, and ignoring reminders cancels nothing).
6. A colleague says: "Switch on 'Workshop starting soon' so everyone who paid gets reminded on Friday."
   - Right answer: she cannot switch it on herself. She asks a super admin to switch it on, 1 day before. On the day itself, she can also use "Email check-in details to attendees" in the Check-in link and QR panel.
   - Why: once a super admin switches it on, each paid attendee gets one 'Tomorrow: <workshop title> starts at <HH:MM>' email. It goes to paid bookings only, once per address, and never to a school's booking address. Anyone who books after the reminder moment does not get it.
   - Tempting wrong answers: ticking 'On' herself (a creator cannot save it). Asking Finance to use "Remind" (that is for unpaid EFT bookings only). Using 'Send an EFT payment reminder now' (Finance-only, and it lists unpaid EFT bookings only). Expecting it to reach the people who still owe money (it goes to paid bookings only).
7. A school bursar emails: "We will pay half of our booking now and the rest next month. Please record the first half."
   - Right answer: explain there are no part payments. The booking stays unpaid until the full amount is in. Pass it to Finance.
   - Why: the system cannot record part payments. Reminders continue until Finance marks it paid in full. Finance or the Provincial CEO decides any exception.
   - Tempting wrong answers: asking Finance to mark it paid when half arrives ("Mark EFT paid" always records the full booked amount, so it would record a payment that did not happen). Halving the workshop price (existing bookings keep their booked amount, and everyone booking after would pay less).
8. Finance marked a member's EFT paid this morning. She phones: "I never got my receipt." Her row is in "Check in" and "View details" shows 'Sent · Invoice and receipt' to her correct address.
   - Right answer: ask her to check her spam folder, use "Resend receipt email", and if it says already delivered, send her the file from "Download receipt (PDF)".
   - Why: both buttons are hers to use. A delivered receipt is not sent twice, so the PDF download is the backup.
   - Tempting wrong answers: asking Finance to press "Mark EFT paid" again (the server refuses a second payment, and a paid mark must never be repeated). Asking Finance for a "Remind" email (reminders are for unpaid bookings and carry no receipt). Saying a copy cannot be made (any staff member can download the receipt PDF from the row's "…" menu).
9. A member with an unpaid EFT booking phones. She has not paid yet. "Your email has a 'Pay by card instead' button. I clicked it and the page said 'Welcome back. Your details are still saved, so you only need to pay.' Did I make a second booking? Should I just pay by card?"
   - Right answer: it is the same booking, not a new one. On this workshop the card option is only a demonstration, so she pays by EFT with the EFT reference from the email, and pays once.
   - Why: "Pay by card instead" and the other pay links in the booking emails reopen the booking she already has. That is why the page says 'Welcome back'. The same goes for the one 'Your workshop booking is not paid yet - <title>' email ('Complete your payment') that goes to someone who chose card and stopped. That is the 'Card checkout abandoned' rule at work. On ordinary workshops the button reads 'Card (demonstration)' and says 'NO REAL MONEY MOVES'. Reminders stop once Finance marks it paid.
   - Tempting wrong answers: paying by card (on ordinary workshops card is a demonstration: 'No money moves and nothing is charged, the receipt says so.' The page would show her as paid, but NAPTOSA gets nothing). Saying she made a second booking and asking Finance to cancel one (there is only one booking). Telling her to start again for a fresh EFT reference (her booking and reference are still valid).

What she cannot do, and what happens instead:

- Switch on the 'Workshop starting soon' email or the two attendance emails. They are switched on in "Reminder settings" by a super admin, case admin, admin, supervisor or Finance. In the Western Cape that means the super admins. All three are off until someone switches them on. Instead: she asks a super admin. Once 'Workshop starting soon' is on, each paid attendee gets one email before the workshop. On the workshop day, a creator can also open the Check-in link and QR panel, click "Email check-in details to attendees" (the 'Workshop starts today' email), then 'Send to N attendees'. That one only works on the workshop's own day.
- Send a payment reminder by hand ("Remind" on the row, or 'Send an EFT payment reminder now' on the "Reminders" tab). Both are Finance-only and hidden from a creator. Each person can get one reminder by hand per South African calendar day. A second try that day says the person 'already had a reminder sent today. Nobody was emailed again.' Instead: ask Finance to send one. Meanwhile give the member the EFT reference and the details she can read in "View details", in the Email row, under 'Preview <type> email'.
- Send a reminder to a different email, or stop reminders for one person. A reminder always goes to the payer's booking email, and nobody can change who it goes to. There is no switch per person or per workshop. The schedule is shared by every province. Instead: for a wrong address, Finance uses "Correct payer email" in "View details". Reminders stop when Finance marks the booking paid, when Finance cancels the unpaid booking, when registrations close, or when the workshop starts.
- Undo a "Mark EFT paid" done by mistake. Invoice and receipt numbers are issued and the receipt goes at once. No button reverses it. Instead: never mark paid until the bank statement shows the money. If a mistake happens, stop and escalate to the super admin and the Provincial CEO. Do not try to fix it in the app.
- Record a part payment. A booking is only paid for the full amount it was booked at, in one go. Instead: the booking stays in "Owes money" and reminders continue until the full amount is in. Pass the details to Finance and escalate if the payer insists.
- Refund a member, or cancel a booking that is already paid. There is no refund in the system, a paid row has no "Cancel booking", and the server refuses: 'This booking is paid. Paid bookings need a refund and cannot be cancelled here.' Instead: escalate to Finance and the Provincial CEO. A refund is a bank transfer made outside the app.
- Move a booking to another person. Once EFT instructions have gone out, staff cannot change the name, the number of places or the payer type on a booking. Only the email can be corrected, and only by Finance. Instead: Finance cancels the unpaid booking, and the colleague books her own place on the same link (Published Links, then "Application link", then "Copy link") and pays herself.
- Re-send an email that was already delivered (EFT instructions or the receipt). She gets '<Type> email was already delivered, so it was not sent again.' Instead: for a receipt, use "Download receipt (PDF)" and send the file by hand. For bank details, read them from 'Preview <type> email', or ask Finance for a reminder by hand, which carries the same details.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block.

If you are running late: Ask only the first question (the "I did the EFT yesterday" call) and the third (the "just log in as me" colleague). The rest go into her workbook.

PART 5: CHANGES, PROBLEMS AND WHAT TO ESCALATE (11:10 TO 11:50)

This part is about what happens after a workshop is live. Venues fall through, dates move, prices go up, and members type their email wrong or can't come. Most of these calls reach her first. Half the fixes belong to Finance, not to her.

So the question for the whole part stays the same: is this mine to do, or do I recognise it and hand it to Finance?

"Finance" means anyone with the Finance tick, the auditor role or a super admin. In the Western Cape nobody has the Finance tick and there is no auditor, so Finance here is the super admins only. Today that is you, on your second screen.

11:10 · Change a live workshop, then close and reopen it (2 min)

Goal: She learns that she can change the price of a workshop after people have booked, and that the people already booked keep the amount they booked at. She also now knows that closing a workshop is safe: it switches off the booking link, deletes nothing, and can be reopened.

Who drives: Her screen. You watch.

Before you start:
- She works on the workshop she created this morning:
  - Title: TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after
  - Short code: TCDDA2
- Sipho Ndlovu's booking is on it. She made it at 09:47 as a non-member paying by EFT, with her own email, so his EFT instructions have gone out to her inbox.
  - Email on Sipho's booking: <Trainee work email>
- She must not touch "TRAINING - Conflict Resolution Skills - delete after" or "TRAINING - Positive Discipline Follow-up - delete after". The next two role-plays need them exactly as they are. If her own workshop has no booking with EFT instructions sent, she tells you.

Two ideas she needs for this block:
- EFT instructions is the email with NAPTOSA's bank details and a payment reference. Once someone presses "Send EFT instructions", the amount they owe is fixed. A later price change only affects new bookings.
- The short code is the code of up to 6 letters the system built from the title when she created the workshop. It goes into every EFT payment reference and receipt number. So Sipho's reference looks like EFT-TCDDA2-<number>-NDLOV.

What happens:
1. She goes to the staff workshop page and opens "Published Links".
2. She picks "TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after" under "Current & upcoming".
3. You ask: "If you raise the price now, what will Sipho owe?" She writes her guess down before she clicks anything.
4. She clicks "Edit details". The form opens with the heading "Edit Workshop".
5. She reads the band that starts "Changing a price or a date affects future registrations only". Point out that there is no Status field in this form.
6. In section "2. Delivery & Pricing" she raises the non-member price by R 50:
   - Field: Non-member price in ZAR
   - Old value: 450
   - New value: 500
7. She clicks "Save changes" and reads the toast. A toast is the short message that pops up on screen after a click.
8. She opens the "Registrations, coupons and documents" section and clicks "Open this workshop's registration desk".
9. She finds Sipho's row, clicks the "…" button on that row and chooses "View details".
10. She reads "Amount / method" and compares it with her guess.
11. Back on "Published Links", with the workshop still selected, she clicks "Close registrations". There is no "are you sure" question. One click closes it.
12. She opens the "Closed archive" group, finds the workshop and reads the "Application link" field. The Application link is the public link people use to book.
13. She clicks "Reopen registrations".
14. She confirms the question that pops up.
15. She writes in her workbook: the toast after saving, which group the workshop sat in, Sipho's "Amount / method", the closing toast word for word, and what the "Application link" field said while it was closed. She does not edit or close the workshop a second time to do this.

She should see:
- After saving: the toast "Workshop updated." She is back on "Published Links" on the same workshop, still under "Current & upcoming", with the new price on the card.
- In "View details": "Amount / method" still shows the amount Sipho booked at (R 450,00 if he booked at the full non-member price). His row still reads "Owes R 450,00 · EFT ref EFT-TCDDA2-<number>-NDLOV" with the same reference as before.
- After closing: the toast "Registrations closed. The application link is inactive." The workshop now sits under "Closed archive".
- The "Application link" field reads "Reopen registrations to activate the application link."
- The reopen question reads: 'Reopen registrations for "TRAINING - Classroom Discipline - delete after"? The public application link will become active and people can register again.'
- After reopening: the toast "Workshop link is active. Registrations are open." The workshop is back under "Current & upcoming" and "Copy link" works again.

If it goes wrong:
- "Member price cannot be more than the standard (non-member) price.": she makes "Member price in ZAR" the same as or lower than "Non-member price in ZAR", then clicks "Save changes" again.
- "Use a valid date range where the end is after the start.": she fixes the End date so it is after the Start.
- The workshop jumped to "Needs closure" and "Copy link" and "Open link" are greyed out: she saved an End date in the past. "Needs closure" is the group for workshops whose end date has passed but whose booking link is still open. She clicks "Edit details" again, fixes the dates and saves.
- She asks whether a finished workshop closes by itself: yes, at 00:05 the night after it ends. The website's Events page drops a workshop once its end time passes. The booking link still takes bookings from anyone who has the link or the QR code until someone clicks "Close registrations" or that night's run closes it. Ended workshops wait in "Needs closure" until then. If she reopens a workshop whose dates have passed, the next 00:05 run closes it again, so if the event moved she edits the dates instead.
- "Workshop changes could not be saved." followed by a reason: nothing was saved. She reads the reason, fixes it and clicks "Save changes" again.
- She clicked "Cancel edit": nothing was saved. She opens "Edit details" again.
- She pressed Enter in the venue box: that only adds a new line. It does not save.
- She reaches for the red "Delete workshop" button: stop her. It is not for taking a workshop off sale. It deletes the workshop, its link, its files and every booking on it for good, without emailing anyone, and it only works while nobody has paid. Sipho hasn't paid, so here it would work. Closing registrations is how you take a workshop off sale.

Cannot be undone: Nothing in this block. The price change only affects new bookings and can be edited back. Closing and reopening can both be reversed. No email goes out. The workshop must end this block reopened, because it stays open until the clean-up at the end of the day.

If you are running late: Skip closing and reopening. She does them for real in the capstone at 12:27. Keep only the price change and the "View details" check.

11:12 · The phone call from the Rietvlei bursar (13 min)

Goal: She learns that she can move a workshop's date, venue and price after people have booked, and that anyone who already got EFT instructions keeps their old amount. She realises that saving the change tells nobody, so she has to email people herself. She now knows that changing the number of people on a booking is not possible for her: Finance cancels the booking and the school books again.

Who drives: Her screen, in the real staff app, while she talks to you on the "phone". Your second screen (Finance) only comes in if you choose to run the cancel at the very end.

Before you start:
- The workshop is selected in "Published Links" > "Current & upcoming". Right now it looks like this:
  - Title: TRAINING - Conflict Resolution Skills - delete after
  - Venue and address: TRAINING ONLY, not a real event (Training Room 1)
  - Start: 14 Nov 2026 08:30
  - End: 14 Nov 2026 12:30
  - Non-member price: R 350
  - Member price: R 250
  - Capacity: 30
  - CPTD points: 5
  - Short code: the one you noted in pre-flight (expected TCRSDA)
- The short code is the code of up to 6 letters the system built from the title. It goes into every EFT payment reference, so each booking's reference starts EFT-TCRSDA.
- Two unpaid EFT bookings sit on it. EFT instructions is the email with NAPTOSA's bank details and a payment reference. Both bookings have had theirs sent, so their amounts are fixed. Neither is paid.
  - Booking 1:
    - Booked by: Rietvlei Primary School, 3 members and 2 non-members
    - Email: loxly@lbtec.co.za
    - Owes: R 1 450,00, reference ending RIETV
  - Booking 2:
    - Booked by: Lungile Mkhize, member
    - Email: loxly@lbtec.co.za
    - Owes: R 250,00, reference ending MKHIZ
- The new Bellville poster (a portrait picture file under 25 MB, with "bellville" in its name) is on her desktop.
- She is signed in as a Workshop creator with the Finance tick off. The Finance tick is the setting that gives a staff account the money buttons: mark paid, cancel, remind and correct an email. Your Finance screen is on the second screen.
- The hand-off sentence card lies face down on the table.
- As Coach, read her brief aloud: "You are the Workshop creator. You do not have the Finance tick. The Provincial CEO has asked you to move "TRAINING - Conflict Resolution Skills - delete after" to Bellville Teachers' Centre, 12 Voortrekker Road, Bellville, 7530, on 21 Nov 2026 from 09:00 to 13:00. Raise the non-member price to R 400. Members stay at R 250. Use the new poster on your desktop. A school bursar who booked last week is about to phone. Work in the app while you talk."
- Say the warning now, before the scene starts: "When you save, the old poster is gone for good." If you plan to run the Finance cancel at the end, also say: "That cancel deletes the booking for good and sends a cancellation email."

You play: Mrs Fatima Salie, the bursar at Rietvlei Primary School in Athlone. She handles the school's payments and leave paperwork. Organised, polite and a little rushed. She talks in short, practical sentences and wants clear answers she can take to her principal. She booked 5 places for her staff by EFT last week and hasn't paid yet. She's heard the November workshop is moving, and she's seen a higher price on the website.

Open with: "Hi, it's Fatima from Rietvlei Primary. I heard the November workshop is moving. Is it still in Training Room 1 on the 14th?"

Keep these to yourself until she asks:
- She asks whether you've paid or what you've received: you have last week's EFT instructions email with the reference on it, and you planned to pay this Friday.
- She asks why you need it in writing, or by when: your principal needs the new venue and date in writing by tomorrow to sign leave forms.
- She explains that the booking must be cancelled and made again: the sixth teacher only decided this morning, and you don't mind a new reference as long as you're told clearly.

Stay inside these lines as Fatima:
- Never pay the EFT and never read out real bank or card details.
- Never give a real ID, PERSAL, NAP or cell number. Use placeholder values if she asks.
- Only use loxly@lbtec.co.za.
- If she promises an automatic email, accept it in character. Raise it in the debrief.
- Stay polite. Mild impatience at most.

What happens:

Part 1: Where and when now?
1. You say, as Narrator: "You hear typing on the other end of the line." Then, as Fatima, the opening line. Let her put you on hold while she works.
2. She clicks "Edit details" and reads the band at the top.
3. In section "1. Details & Scheduling" she moves the date:
   - Start, old: 14 Nov 2026 08:30
   - Start, new: 21 Nov 2026 09:00
   - End, old: 14 Nov 2026 12:30
   - End, new: 21 Nov 2026 13:00
4. In section "2. Delivery & Pricing" she changes the venue. Pressing Enter in this box only adds a line. It does not save.
   - Venue and address, old: TRAINING ONLY, not a real event (Training Room 1)
   - Venue and address, new: Bellville Teachers' Centre, 12 Voortrekker Road, Bellville, 7530
5. In the same section she raises the non-member price and leaves the member price alone:
   - Non-member price in ZAR, old: 350
   - Non-member price in ZAR, new: 400
   - Member price in ZAR: stays 250
6. In section "4. Documents & Media" she clicks "Browse files" and picks the new Bellville poster from the desktop.
   - Poster, old: the original Conflict Resolution poster
   - Poster, new: the Bellville poster
7. She checks that the saved poster now reads "Public poster · replaced when you save changes".
8. She clicks "Preview" and checks the card shows the new venue, date and price. She closes it with "Hide preview", the X ("Close preview") or Escape.
9. She clicks "Save changes" and reads the toast. A toast is the short message that pops up after a click.
10. She checks the card is still under "Current & upcoming" with the 21 Nov dates.
11. She tells Fatima the new venue and date, read off the saved card and not from memory.

Watch for: she doesn't go looking for a Status field, she doesn't press Enter in the venue box to save, she checks the poster in "Workshop files", and she uses "Preview". "Workshop files" is the list of every picture and document saved on this workshop. If she clicks "Cancel edit" by mistake, let the old poster stay and see whether she checks.

Part 2: Do we owe more now?

12. You say, as Fatima: "Okay. And I saw on the website the price is now R400 for non-members. We haven't paid our EFT yet, so do our two non-members now owe R400 each?"
13. She opens "Registrations, coupons and documents" and clicks "Open this workshop's registration desk".
14. She finds the Rietvlei Primary School row, clicks the "…" button and chooses "View details".
15. She reads "Amount / method".
16. She tells Fatima that the amount and the reference haven't changed, because the amount was fixed when the EFT instructions went out.
17. She does the same "View details" check on Lungile Mkhize's row.

If she answers from memory without opening "View details", you ask as Fatima: "Are you sure? Can you check?" If she says R 400 each, accept it, sounding worried. Don't correct her in character. Take it to the debrief.

Part 3: Put it in writing

18. You say, as Fatima: "Can you send me something in writing with the new venue and date? Our principal needs it for the leave forms."
19. She tells Fatima that the system doesn't email anyone about the change, and that she'll email Fatima herself from the office mailbox.
20. She goes to "All Registrations" and clicks "Export <N> (CSV)", where N is the number of bookings shown. This downloads a spreadsheet of the bookings. Her copy has no money columns, but it does have the Email column. Reading Fatima's email in "View details" also counts.
21. She thinks of the other person on this workshop, Lungile Mkhize, who also needs to hear about the change.

The big catch here is a false promise like "you'll get an email from the system". If she makes it, Fatima says "Great, I'll watch for it" and moves on. Note whether she remembers Lungile without a prompt. Optional: she can open "Preview eft instructions email" to see what Fatima already has.

Part 4: One more teacher

22. If she's new to this, model the wrong move first, as Coach: "So I just add one on the booking, right?" Let her stop you.
23. You say, as Fatima: "Oh, and one more teacher wants to come. Can you make our booking 6 people instead of 5? We'll just add it to the same EFT."
24. She says she can't change the number of people on a booking once the EFT instructions have gone out. No staff screen has a headcount field, so she doesn't go looking for one.
25. She names Finance as the person who cancels the unpaid booking.
26. She tells Fatima to book again for 6 people on the same link, and that a new reference will come in the new EFT instructions email.
27. Only if time allows: you, as Finance on your screen, click "Cancel booking" on the Rietvlei row and pick the reason. She watches the row disappear.
    - Reason: Booked by mistake

What a good hand-off sounds like (the card she turns over if she needs it): "Mrs Salie, I can't change the number of people on a booking once the EFT instructions have gone out. I'll ask our Finance colleague to cancel this booking today. You'll get a cancellation email saying no payment is due, and then please book again for 6 people on the same link. Your new EFT reference will be in the new EFT instructions email."

She should see:
- The toast "Workshop updated." The card under "Current & upcoming" shows the 21 Nov 2026 dates, "R 400,00" and the line "Members R 250,00".
- "Workshop files" lists the new picture as "Public poster · shown on the booking page". The old poster is gone. Above the list sits the note "The poster shows on the booking page only. To take a workshop off the website’s Events page, use Close registrations." Each file has "Download" and "Remove". She doesn't click "Remove".
- "Open link" shows the public booking page with the Bellville venue, "R 400,00" and "NAPTOSA members R 250,00".
- The Rietvlei row still starts "Owes R 1 450,00 · EFT ref" with the same reference ending RIETV. In "View details", "Amount / method" shows "R 1 450,00" with "3 members, 2 non-members (5 total) · EFT" under it.
- Lungile's row still starts "Owes R 250,00 · EFT ref" with the reference ending MKHIZ. Her "Amount / method" shows "R 250,00" with "Member rate · EFT" under it.
- After the export, a spreadsheet file downloads with "no-financial-data" in its name. No toast appears for this.

If she gets stuck: Wait for a real stall: she says she's stuck, asks for help, or sits for about a minute without a move. First point her at the face-down hint slip. Then give these one at a time.
- Part 1: "Say what you will check before you answer, then check the saved workshop, not your memory."
- Part 1: "After saving, look at where the card sits and what it shows."
- Part 2: "The question is about her booking, not about the workshop. Where do you see one booking's amount?"
- Part 2: "Before you answer, decide whether a price change can reach a booking that already has EFT instructions."
- Part 3: "Ask yourself: when you pressed Save, who was told?"
- Part 3: "Is Fatima the only person on this workshop who needs to know?"
- Part 4: "Decide first: is changing a booking something your account can do, or something you hand on?"
- Part 4: "Tell her what will happen next, in order, and what she will receive."
